एण्ड हॉस्पिटल, लखनऊ के एमडीएस छात्रों को प्रदान की गई दंत चिकित्सा डिग्री की मान्यता से संबंधित दंत चिकित्सक 
अधिनियम, 1948 (1948 का 16) की अनुसूची के भाग- 1 में क्रम संख्या 156 के सामने कॉलम 2 और 3 की मौजूदा 
प्रविष्टियों में निम्नलिखित प्रविष्टियां अंतःस्थापित की जाएंगी, अर्थात्‌:

S.O. 1616.—In exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section (2) of section 10 of the Dentists Act, 1948

following amendments in Part-I of the Schedule to the said Act, namely:-

Act, 1948 (16 of 1948) pertaining to recognition of Dental Degrees awarded by Atal Bihari Vajpayee Medical

Hospital, Lucknow the following entries shall be inserted, namely:

का.आ. 1617.--केन्द्रीय सरकार, दंत चिकित्सक अधिनियम, 1948 (1948 का 16) की धारा 10 की उपधारा 


(2) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए, भारतीय दंत चिकित्सा परिषद के परामर्श के पश्चात, उक्त अधिनियम की 
अनुसूची के भाग- में एतदद्वारा निम्नलिखित संशोधन करती है, अर्थात्‌ :- 

पैसिफिक मेडिकल यूनिवर्सिटी, उदयपुर द्वारा पैसिफिक डेन्टल कॉलेज एण्ड रिसर्च सेंटर, उदयपुर के एमडीएस 
छात्रों को प्रदान की गई दंत चिकित्सा डिग्री की मान्यता से संबंधित दंत चिकित्सक अधिनियम, 1948 (1948 का 16) की 


अनुसूची के भाग- में क्रम सख्या 139 के सामने कॉलम 2 और 3 की मौजूदा प्रविष्टियों में निम्नलिखित प्रविष्टियां 
अंतःस्थापित की जाएंगी, अर्थात्‌: 


पैसिफिक डेनूटल कॉलेज एणूड रिसर्च सेंटर, उदयपुर 


ओरल मेडिसिन एण्ड रेडियोलॉजी एमडीएस (ओरल मेडिसिन एण्ड रेडियोलॉजी) 
(2 सीटों के साथ, यदि 25.09.2024 को या उसके बाद पैसिफिक मेडिकल यूनिवर्सिटी, उदयपुर 

स्वीकृत की जाती है)। 

कंजर्वेटिव डेंटिस्ट्री एण्ड एंडोडोंटिक्स एमडीएस (कंजर्वेटिव डेंटिस्ट्री एण्ड एंडोडोंटिक्स) 
(3 सीटों के साथ, यदि 28.09.2024 को या उसके बाद पैसिफिक मेडिकल यूनिवर्सिटी, उदयपुर 

स्वीकृत की जाती है)। 


[भाग ए---खण्ड 361)] भारत का राजपत्र : सितम्बर 13, 2025/ भाद्र 22, 1947 6011 


पेरियोडॉन्टोलॉजी wading (पेरियोडॉन्टोलॉजी) 

(2 सीटों के साथ, यदि 28.09.2024 को या उसके बाद पैसिफिक मेडिकल यूनिवर्सिटी, उदयपुर 

स्वीकृत की जाती है)। 

प्रोस्थोडोंटिक्स एण्ड क्राउन एंड ब्रिज एमडीएस (प्रोस्थोडोंटिक्स एण्ड क्राउन एंड ब्रिज) 
(3 सीटों के साथ, यदि 25.09.2024 को या उसके बाद पैसिफिक मेडिकल यूनिवर्सिटी, उदयपुर 

स्वीकृत की जाती है)।

का.आ. 1618.--केंद्रीय सरकार, दंत चिकित्सक अधिनियम, 1948 (1948 का 16) की धारा 10 की उपधारा 
(2) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए भारतीय दंत परिषद के परामर्श के पश्चात, उक्त अधिनियम की अनुसूची के 
भाग- में एतद्दारा निम्नलिखित संशोधन करती है, अर्थात्‌:-

के एमडीएस छात्रों को प्रदान की गई दंत डिग्री की मान्यता से संबंधित दंत चिकित्सक अधिनियम, 1948 (1948 का 16)

अंतःस्थापित की जाएंगी, अर्थातः

का.आ. 1619.--केन्द्रीय सरकार, दंत चिकित्सक अधिनियम, 1948 (1948 का 16) की धारा 10 की उपधारा 
(2) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए, भारतीय दंत चिकित्सा परिषद के परामर्श के पश्चात, उक्त अधिनियम की 
अनुसूची के भाग- में एतदद्वारा निम्नलिखित संशोधन करती है, अर्थात्‌ :- 


कलोजी नारायण राव स्वास्थ्य विज्ञान विश्वविद्यालय,वारंगल, तेलंगाना द्वारा तिरुमाला दंत चिकित्सा विज्ञान 
संस्थान एवं अनुसंधान केंद्र, निज़ामाबाद, तेलंगाना के बीडीएस छात्रों को प्रदान की गई दंत चिकित्सा डिग्री की मान्यता से 
संबंधित दंत चिकित्सक अधिनियम, 1948 (1948 का 16) की अनुसूची के भाग- में क्रम संखया 141 के सामने कॉलम 2 
और 3 की मौजूदा प्रविष्टियों में निम्नलिखित प्रविष्टियां अंतःसथापित की जाएंगी, अर्थात्‌: 


तिरुमाला दंत चिकित्सा विज्ञान संस्थान एवं अनुसंधान केंद्र, निज़ामाबाद, तेलंगाना 


बैचलर ऑफ डेंटल सर्जरी बीडीएस, 


(100 सीटों के साथ, यदि 05.02.2025 को या उसके बाद 
स्वीकृत की जाती है)। 


कलोजी नारायण राव स्वास्थ्य विज्ञान विश्वविद्यालय, 
वारंगल, तेलंगाना

का.आ. 1620.--केंद्रीय सरकार एतद्दवारा राजभाषा (संघ के शासकीय प्रयोजनों के लिए प्रयोग) नियम, 1976 
(1987 में यथा संशोधित) के नियम 10 के उप नियम (4) के अनुसरण में, कोयला मंत्रालय के नियंत्रणाधीन कार्यालय 
“कोयला नियंत्रक संगठन (सीसीओ)', cere मीनार, पॉचवीं मंजिल, कोर-।, लक्ष्मी नगर, दिलली-110092, जिसके 
80% से अधिक कर्मचारियों ने हिंदी का कार्यसाधक ज्ञान प्राप्त कर लिया है, को अधिसूचित करती है। 


[फा. सं. 13012/1/2017-हिंदी] 


आस्था जैन, संयुक्त निदेशक 


MINISTRY OF COAL 
New Delhi, the 3rd September, 2025 


S.O. 1620.—In pursuance of sub Rule (4) of Rule 10 of the Official Languages (Use for Official purposes of 
the Union) Rules, 1976 (as amended in 1987), Central Government hereby notifies the Office ‘Coal Controller's 
Organisation (CCO)’, Scope Minar, Fifth Floor, Core-I, Lakshmi Nagar, Delhi-110092 under the control of Ministry 
of Coal located at Delhi, wherein more than 80% of the staff have acquired working Knowledge of Hindi. 


[F. No. 13012/1/2017-HINDI] 
AASTHA JAIN, Jt. Director 


नई दिल्‍ली, 3 सितम्बर, 2025 


का.आ. 1621.--केंद्रीय सरकार एतद्ववारा राजभाषा (संघ के शासकीय प्रयोजनों के लिए प्रयोग) नियम, 1976 


(1987 में यथा संशोधित) के नियम 10 के उप नियम (4) के अनुसरण में, कोयला मंत्रालय के नियंत्रणाधीन नॉर्दर्न 
कोलफील्ड्स लिमिटेड के दो क्षेत्रीय कार्यालयों नामत: ब्लॉक-बी क्षेत्रीय कार्यालय एवं कृष्णशिला क्षेत्रीय कार्यालय, जिनके 
80% से अधिक कर्मचारियों ने हिंदी का कार्यसाधक ज्ञान प्राप्त कर लिया है, को अधिसूचित करती है। 


[फा. सं. 13012/1/2017-हिंदी] 


आस्था जैन, संयुक्त निदेशक

Government hereby publishes the award (Reference No. 18/2018) of the Central Government Industrial Tribunal

6016 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA :SEPTEMBER 13, 2025/BHADRA 22, 1947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


ANNEXURE 
BEFORE THE 
CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIUBNAL-CUM-LABOUR COURT

DATED : 17" JULY 2025 
PRESENT : SMT. K P INDIRA 
Presiding Officer 
ILD No. 18/2018 
___I Party ___ । Party 
Kum. Asha L, 


The Senior Divisional Manager, 


पी  लबफ ज pve, LIC of India, Divisional Office, 
Post : Hoasabelvanur, 100" Feet Road, 
Tal & Dist: DAVANGERE 577 002. Gopaldeowda Rudd, 
SHIVAMOGGA - 577 205. 
Appearances 
I Party : Shri M Rama Rao 
Authorized Representative 
II Party : Shri Rajesh Shetty 
Advocate 
1. This is a Petition filed under Section 2A of the ID act. The claim set forth by the 1“ party workman is that 


she was appointed as an Assistant by the 2™ Party in a clear vacancy of Office Assistant w.e.f. 18.07.2013 by 
obtaining an Application and she has attended the interview conducted by the II Party; she was also assured with 
regularization of her services for the services rendered by her and that she was doing the duties of regular staff; that 
she was not paid with minimum scales as applicable to the Assistants and took work for more than eight hours per day 
without paying her overtime wages; inspite of repeated requests to absorb her to the permanent post she was deprived 
with the benefits of the permanent employees; that she was not provided with Provident Fund, Bonus Benefits though 
other employees were extended with the same; that she was suddenly retrenched from the services of the II Party 
orally from 16.12.2017 in the morning hours by the Branch Manager without notice, without payment of notice pay / 
retrenchment compensation though there were no allegations against her. She was retrenched from the services 
without prior intimation or paying her retrenchment compensation and without following due process of law and that 
the II Party has retrenched more than 20 workmen without filing approval application seeking permission from the 
appropriate Government to retrench their services as mandated under Section 25N of the Act; that due to the 
retrenchment she is facing irreparable hardship, agony and unable to run her family and that she does not know any 
other work than the Assistant job; that the II Party has employed fresh entrants in her place and the nature of work 
done by her is perennial in nature and taking the new entrants is illegal and malafide; that she raised an industrial 
dispute since no settlement was arrived she preferred to file the present petition requesting this Tribunal to declare the 
retrenchment of service by the II Party as not in accordance with the provisions of ID Act; reinstate her into service 
with full back wages and other benefits, regularization with continuity of service and ancillary benefits from the date 
of her appointment 18.07.2013 with cost. 


2. The 2" Party in their Counter Statement rebutted the Claim Statement stating that all the averments made are 
false; that LIC of India (Staff) Regulations, 1960 are not applicable to the temporary employees appointed by the II 
Party; that the I Party is put to strict proof of the above averments; that she was doing some unspecified job on daily 
wages on need basis and that they were paying wages as per the notification issued by the Government of Karnataka 
from time to time and therefore the question of extending Provident Fund Benefits does not arise. As the specified 
job was completed, her services were disengaged as per law and the question of issuing notice, retrenchment 
compensation or notice pay does not arise and that the II Party has not contravened the provisions of ID Act and the 
question of reinstatement with the back wages and other benefits does not arise. The Respondent has prayed for 
dismissal of the Petition with cost. 


[भाग ए---खण्ड 361)] भारत का राजपत्र : सितम्बर 13, 2025/ भाद्र 22, 1947 6017 


3. On the rival pleadings the following points arise for consideration: 


“01. Whether the I Party has succeeded in proving that she was appointed by the II Party as Assistant 
on temporary basis in a clear vacancy as contended? 


02. Whether the disengagement of the I Party from job by the II Party is illegal as contended by the I 
Party? 


03. Whether the I Party is entitled for the reliefs prayed for? 
04. What is the proper order to be passed?” 


4. The I party has got examined herself as WW | and Ex W-1 to Ex W-9 are got marked. From the side of the 
II Party MW 1 is examined and Ex M-1 is marked. 

5. Heard the arguments advanced by both sides. Written notes of Arguments are also submitted by both sides. 
6. POINTS NO. 1 TO 3: Since the matter to be considered and adjudicated on these points are intertwined 


and interwoven, all these points are discussed together. 


7. The I Party WW | claimed herself as a temporary employee / Assistant in Class III Category in a clear 
vacancy of Office Assistant under the II Party LIC of India since 18.07.2013 at its Davanagere II Branch and 
continued to work for Four years till 16.12.2017. Her case is that she has put in an application before the II Party and 
she was interviewed by the officials of the LIC at the Branch Office. According to her she was appointed with an 
assurance of regularization of her service within a few years coupled with the benefits applicable to permanent 
Assistants. The above claim of the I Party is stoutly denied by the II Party on the precincts that the I Party was 
engaged by the LIC purely on daily wages to perform some unspecified jobs and she has been paid daily wage for the 
days of her engagement. Such payment according to the II Party has never been meant as “SALARY”. 


8. Among the documents produced and proved by WW 1, there is nothing indicting her to have been called for 
an interview for the post of Office Assistant. She did not produce copy of application said to have been submitted by 
her before the II Party to the said post. Though she claimed that the II Party did publish notification calling for 
applications on their notice board, she could not specify the date of notification or its exhibition on the notice board. 
She was not issued with any appointment letter by the II Party. On meticulous cross-examination she testified that 
she was appointed on temporary basis as daily wager. She was given wages for the period she served in the II Party. 
Ex W-1 series are a voucher dated 18.12.2017 and its corresponding receipt produced by the I Party to prove the 
payment received by her from the II Party LIC. The voucher reflects the wage received by the I Party and similarly 
placed other workers. This voucher would never improve her case, much less it substantiates her contention that she 
was appointed in a clear vacancy by undergoing regular recruitment process. This voucher would never indicate 
continuous and uninterrupted service of the I Party under the II Party for Four years as contended by her. 


9. MW 1 the Divisional Manager of the II Party specifically deposed that there is no particular notification for 
LIC separately for daily wage employees and such daily wagers were engaged based on need and requirements and 
there is no prescribed recruitment procedure for engaging them. Their wages were paid monthly in cash, debited from 
imprest Cash Account No. 111772. This fact stood revealed from Ex W-1 series vouchers. MW | reiterated that no 
order is issued to such persons for engaging them on daily wage. So without getting an appointment order from the 
management the I Party worker cannot qualify for herself to be entitled for permanency or rather regularization in the 
job. There is no basis for the so called assurance alleged to have been given by the II Party that she would be given 
permanency in service along with equivalent other benefits. 


10. The purpose of production of Ex W-2 by the I Party, the confidential communication issued by the Regional 
Manager (P&IR) to all SDM under South Central Zones dated 25.11.2017 alerting the divisions to disengage all the 
daily wagers who are continued without any court case and calling for the number of daily wagers continuing by 
virtue of court cases pursuant to the decision of the competent authority is not profitably explained by the I Party. It 
is also not properly explained as to how the I Party / WW | happened to be in custody of Ex W-2, the confidential 
communication addressed to the SDM by its higher-up. Anyhow Ex W-2 is not of any help to the I Party to establish 
her case. 


11. Ex W-3 dated 25.12.2017 is the representation made by the I Party to the Senior Divisional Manager seeking 
regularization of her service being aggrieved by stoppage of her service w.e.f. 23.08.2009 stating the same as 
retrenchment. In Ex W-3 she grieved before the authority that she was pulled out from job without notice and without 
payment of notice pay / retrenchment compensation despite having no allegations against her. 


12. On 27.12.2017 she took up the matter for conciliation before the Assistant Labour Commissioner (C), Hubli 
under Ex W-4. Ex W-5 is the reply submitted by the II Party before the ALC(C), Hubli, in response to the Ex W-4 
representation. All along, the II Party has adopted a firm defence that the I Party was engaged only as a daily wager 
on need basis due to office exigencies and she was neither recruited not given any appointment letter by any authority. 
Her engagement was purely on daily wages for carrying out work on peace meal basis depending upon the needs of 


6018 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA :SEPTEMBER 13, 2025/BHADRA 22, 1947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


the office and not as a temporary employee. Her pay of wages was as per the Government of Karnataka Gazette 
Notification revised from time to time. MW 1 during her evidence precisely testified that since the I Party was paid 
wages counting the number of days she worked, no deduction was made from her wage towards Provident Fund and 
payment of bonus to her also did not arise for consideration. The circumstances explained by MW | substantiates the 
contention raised by the II Party that the I Party was engaged only as a daily wager without there being any assurance 
of permanency of service and such alleged assurance being out of place. 


13. Ex W-6 is the application filed by the I Party before the ALC(C) calling for documents from the II party 
during the conciliation proceedings. As per Ex W-7 the Assistant Labour Commissioner(C) conducted Joint 
Discussion on 05.02.2018 notifying further joint discussion to another date. Ex W-8 is also a similar application filed 
by the I Party before the Conciliation Officer calling for some more documents from the II party during the 
conciliation proceedings. Ex W-9 is the letter issued by the ALC(C) to LEO for collection of photocopies of 
documents from various branches. 


None of the above documents produced and relied upon by the I Party renders any proof to the core point of 
controversy as to the status of employment at the time of her engagement under the II Party. Indeed, had it been an 
engagement as Daily Wager, she does not get entitled for any of the benefits sought in the Petition. The II Party 
Management to buttress its contention that the I Party was simply disengaged as there existed no further requirement 
of her service and she has not been a temporary employee capable of claiming any of the benefits sought for, relied 
upon various judicial pronouncements of the Hon’ble Apex Court. At the threshold it has to be observed that the I 
Party failed in showing any Rule, Law or Regulation nor any administrative order on the basis of which she claims 
reinstatement and regularization with continuity of Service and other monetory benefits. In order to high light the 
said aspect the I Party has relied upon the Hon’ble Supreme Court in Subedar Singh and others vs. District Judge, 
Mirzapur and another 2001 1 SCC 37. 


15. It is contextually significant to follow the dictum laid down by the Hon’ble Apex Court in State of Haryana 
vs. Jasmer Singh (1996 (11) SCC 77) which reads : 


“The respondents, therefore in the present appeals who are employed on daily wages cannot be treated 
as on a par with persons in regular service of the State of Haryana holding similar posts. Daily-rated 
workers are not required to possess the qualifications prescribed for regular workers, nor do they have 
to fulfil the requirement relating to age at the time of recruitment. They are not selected in the manner 
in which regular employees are selected. In other words the requirements for selection are not as 
rigorous. There are also other provisions relating to regular service such as the liability of a member of 
the service to be transferred, and his being subject to the disciplinary jurisdiction of the authorities as 
prescribed, which the daily-rated workmen are not subjected to. They cannot, therefore, be equated 
with regular workmen for the purposes for their wages. Nor can they claim the minimum of the regular 
pay-scale of the regularly employed” 


Referring to the said legal position the Hon’ble Apex Court in State of Haryana and another vs. Tilak Raj and 
others (2003 (6) SCC 123) held that a scale of pay is attached to a definite post and in case of a daily wager, he holds 
no posts. The I Party neither in her pleadings nor in evidence has made out the particular works she performed under 
the II Party, much less any material is placed by her as to the nature of the duties for which she was engaged by the II 
Party. The contention of the II Party in its Statement of Objection that the engagement of the I Party was for meeting 
the office exigencies to attend some unspecified jobs on payment of daily wage for the work performed is thus well 
founded. 


16. In Mohammed Ashif and others vs. State of Bihar and others (2010 (5) SCC 475), the Hon’ble Supreme 
Court held that the question of regularization of services of an Employee may arise only in situations where against an 
available clear vacancy an appointment is made on adhoc or on-daily wage basis by an authority competent to do so 
without any artificial break in service. The second situation in which the regularization could be granted is held to be 
where the initial entry of the employee against an available vacancy was found suffering from some flaws in the 
procedure in making the appointment though the person appointing was competent to make such initial recruitment 
and had otherwise followed the procedure prescribed for such recruitment. It is equally held that “the all-important 
condition precedent for such regularization is that the initial entry of such employee must be made against the 
Sanctioned vacancy and by following the Rules and Regulations governing such entry”. 


17. Similarly the constitution bench of the Hon’ble Apex Court in State of Karnataka vs. Uma Devi (2006 (4) 
SCC 1) held that “/f it is a contractual appointment, the appointment comes to an end at the end of the contract, if it 
were an engagement or appointment on daily wages or casual basis, the same would come to an end when it is 
discontinued. Similarly, a temporary employee could not claim to be made permanent on the expiry of his term of 
appointment. It has also to be clarified that merely because a temporary employee or a casual wage worker is 
continued for a time beyond the term of his appointment, he would not be entitled to be absorbed in regular service or 
made permanent, merely on the strength of such continuance, if the original appointment was not made by following a 
due process of selection as envisaged by the relevant rules. It is not open to the court to prevent regular recruitment 
at the instance of temporary employees whose period of employment has come to an end or of ad hoc employees who 


[भाग ए---खण्ड 361)] भारत का राजपत्र : सितम्बर 13, 2025/ भाद्र 22, 1947 6019 


by the very nature of their appointment, do not acquire any right.” Here in the instant case the I party is claiming 
permanency and seeks for regularization mainly on the so called “long duration of work” (emphasis supplied) and not 
on any other valid ground. The dictum quoted above defeats her claim as it is bereft of valid support and devoid of 
merit 


18. Most of the rulings placed by the I party are not applicable to the facts of the case on hand. The Verdict of 
the Hon’ble High Court of Madras in V Muthukumarasamy and others vs. The Chairman, LIC, Zonal Office 
dated 21.01.2007 also does not get any application in view of the fact involved in that case as the Petitioners therein 
were sponsored by the Employment Exchange and have been appointed on temporary basis from shortlisted 
candidates. As already observed, the I Party herein was not sponsored by Employment Exchange nor her engagement 
was in a clear vacancy by undergoing the Regular Recruitment process. 


19. The I party further relied upon on the ruling of the Hon’ble High Court of Karnataka, Bengaluru in 
R V Mohan Reddy vs. The Chairman, Karnataka Grameena Bank in WP No. 1944/2022 (L-RES) 
dated 10.02.2025 awarding 60% of Backwages to the Employee from the date of his termination reckoning the two 
decades of service he had under the management as a Peon on daily wage. As against that, the principle laid down by 
the Hon’ble Supreme Court in a plethora of rulings aforementioned consistently hold that the Casual Employment 
terminates when the same is discontinued, and merely because a temporary or casual worker has been engaged 
beyond the period of his employment, he would not be entitled to be absorbed into regular service or made 
permanent, if the original appointment was not in terms of the process envisaged by the relevant rules. So the legal 
position settled at rest by the Hon’ble Supreme Court disentitles the I party to put forth any claim of reinstatement, 
regularization, continuity of service or back wages with consequential benefits. 


20. To sum up, the solitary materials viz., Ex W-1 vouchers show only pay for a single month in the respective 
years and there is no valid material proving that her engagement was continuous in nature and she performed duties 
under the II Party continuously till the date of her disengagement so as to show that her tenure of service at a time was 
more than 240 days in a calendar year and her disengagement was in violation of the provisions of Section 25F of the 
ID Act. So on legal principle and on factual matrix the I Party failed to establish a case on merit justifying the relief 
sought for in the instant Industrial Dispute. Eventually her Claim Petition fails and the same is liable to be dismissed. 
With this view Points No. 1103 are answered accordingly. 


21. POINT NO. 4: In view of the above discussion, the I party is held as not entitled for any relief. 
22. In the result, the following Award is passed 
AWARD 
The Claim Petition is Dismissed. No order as to cost. 
(Dictated to Secretary to Court, transcribed by him, corrected and signed by me on 17.07.2025) 
K P INDIRA, Presiding Officer 


नई दिल्‍ली, 8 सितम्बर, 2025

ओम सिंह राजावत पुत्र श्री कल्याण सिंह, माइंस ऑनर के प्रबंधतंत्र के संबद्ध नियोजकों और श्री कैलाश, हिन्द मज़दूर सभा 
के बीच अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट औद्योगिक अधिकरण एवं श्रम न्यायालय, कोटा, पंचाट (रिफरेन्स न.-(केन्द्रीय) 07/2011 
(सीआईएस-97/2014)) को जैसा कि अनुलग्रक में दिखाया गया है, प्रकाशित करती है जो केन्द्रीय सरकार को सॉफ्ट कॉपी 
के साथ 08.09.2025 को प्राप्त हुआ था | 


[सं. एल-29012/31/2005- आई आर (एम)] 
सलोनी, उप निदेशक

भारत सरकार, श्रम मंत्रालय, नई दिल्ली के प्रासांगिक आदेश दिनांक 25.04.2011 के जरिये निर्देश विवाद, औद्योगिक विवाद अधिनियम, 
1947 (जिसे आगे “अधिनियम” से सम्बोधित किया जावेगा) की धारा 10(1)(ध) एवं उपधारा 2(क) के अन्तर्गत इस न्यायाधिकरण को अधिनिर्णयार्थ 
प्राप्त हुआ है:- 


"Whether the claim of Shri Kailash S/o Shri Mana for Rs. 44000/- from Shri om Singh Rajawat S/o Shri 
Kalayan Singh Mines contractor, Sakatpuri, Distt. Kota, Mines situated at Village Rajapur, in lieu of his duty from 25- 
03-2000 to 05-07-2001 is legal and just? If yes, to what relief Sh. Kailash S/o Shri Mana is entitled?" 


2-उक्त विवाद, न्यायाधिकरण में रेफर होने पर पंजीबद्ध कर पक्षकारों को उपस्थिति बाबत नोटिस जारी किए गए। नोटिस की पालना में प्रार्थी श्रमिक 
द्वारा उपस्थित होकर स्टेटमेन्ट ऑफ क्लेम न्यायाधिकरण के समक्ष प्रस्तुत कर संक्षिप्तत: यह कथन किया गया है कि उसे अप्रार्थी ओम सिंह राजावत 
माईन्स लीज होल्डर राजपुरा, जिलाबूंदी, निवासी सकतपुरा, कोटा ने दि. 25.03.2000 से राजपुरा AQ पर स्टोन कटर का कार्य करने हेतु सेवा में 
नियोजित किया। श्रमिक जितना काम रोजाना करता था, नियोजक का मुंशी रोजाना उसकी पर्चीया लिखकर देता था उनमें से अधिकांश पर्चीया 
श्रमिक के पास मौजूद है, इसके अतिरिक्त नियोजक ने श्रमिक को 3750 /-रू. मासिक वेतन देना तय किया हुआ था। श्रमिक महिने के अंत में वेतन 
की मांग करता था परन्तु श्रमिक को नियोजक यह कहता था कि हिसाब बाद में कर लेंगे, गुजारे के लिए पैसे लेते रहो, श्रमिक को 1000/-w. प्रति 
महिने गुजारे खर्चे का दिया जाता था। श्रमिक व उसके भाई ने जब वेतन का हिसाब करने की मांग की तो नियोजक स्वयं ने व इसके द्वारा पाले हुए 
असामाजिक तत्व रघुनाथ मुंशी व अन्य दो व्यक्तियों ने श्रमिक व श्रमिक के भाई के साथ मारपीट की ओर काम से भगा दिया। श्रमिक व उसका भाई 
दिनांक 16.10.2004 को पुलिस अधीक्षक बूंदी को पुलिस अधीक्षक बूंदी को नियोजक व उसके द्वारा पाले हुए असामाजिक तत्वों के विरूद्ध कार्यवाही 
की, जिसकी जांच हेतु पुलिस स्टेशन डाबी, जिला बूंदी में प्रार्थना पत्र भेजा गया तो नियोजक ने पुलिस में गलत बयानी कर दी कि श्रमिक व उसका 
भाई गत 4 माह से फरार है, जबकि श्रमिक व उसका भाई राजपुरा माईन्स से 5 किलोमीटर राज्या का तालाब में निवास कर रहे है, क्योंकि नियोजक 
व उसके पाले हुए असामाजिक तत्व आए दिन उनसे मारपीट करते है, इस कारण WAI AT व इसके आस-पास श्रमिक व उसके भाई का कार्य 
करना मुश्किल हो गया तथा नियोजक श्रमिक व उसके भाई को किसी दूसरी जगह भी काम नहीं करना देता है। श्रमिक ने दि. 18.11.2004 को पुनः 
पुलिस अधीक्षक बूंदी को शिकायत की है जिसकी जांच विचाराधीन है। दि. 25.03.2000 से 05.07.2001 तक का वेतन रूपये 3750/- प्रतिमाह से 
50000 /-रू. बनता है जिसमें से नियोजक ने श्रमिक को 1000/-रू. प्रतिमाह के हिसाब से 16000/-रू. खर्चा चलाने के लिए दिए है 
44000 /-रू. की राशि वेतन लेना शेष है, जिसे नियोजक ने बावजूद तकाजों के नहीं दिया है और मारपीट करने पर आमादा है। श्रमिक का वेतन 
3750 /-रू. प्रतिमाह होने के कारण श्रमिक का ae वेतन भुगतान अधिनियम 1936 के अन्तर्गत नहीं किया जा सकता है क्योंकि अधिनियम के 
अन्तर्गत 1600 /-रू. प्रतिमाह वेतन प्राप्त करने वाला कर्मचारी ही क्लेम कर सकता है, इससे अधिक वेतन प्राप्त करने वालों के लिए इस न्यायालय 
को श्रवणाधिकार प्राप्त नहीं है। अधिनियम की धारा 33 सी() के अन्तर्गत भी श्रम न्यायालय अथवा औद्योगिक न्यायाधिकरण वेतन संबंध क्लेम को

जब तक नहीं लेते है जब तक कि इस संबंध में किसी न्यायालय से अवार्ड पारित नहीं हो जाता क्‍योंकि श्रमिक का वेतन नियोजक के यहां 
विवादग्रस्त हो गया है और विवाद का निपटारा अधिनियम के अन्तर्गत श्रम न्यायालय sear ट्रिब्यूनल को रेफरेंस के माध्यम से पारित करके ही किया 
जा सकता है। ऐसी स्थिति में अधिनियम की धारा 33 सी (2) के अन्तर्गत श्रमिक के वेतन का क्लेम सुनने का अधिकार श्रम न्यायालय अथवा 
औद्योगिक न्यायाधिकरण को नहीं है, इस संबंध में माननीय सर्वोच्च न्यायालय का निर्णय म्यूनिसिपल कॉर्पोरेशन बनाम गणेश, रजाक व अन्य 1995(1) 
एल.एल.जे. 395 है और प्रार्थना की है कि प्रार्थी को नियोजक से दि. 25.03.2000 से 05.07.2001 तक का शेष वेतन 44000 ,//-रू. मय खर्चा मुकदमा

3-अप्रार्थी नेयोजक की ओर से उक्त क्लेम का जवाब प्रस्तुत कर यह प्रतिवाद किया गया है कि प्रतिपक्षी की माईन्स में प्रार्थी द्वारा नियमित तौर पर 
कोई कार्य नहीं किया है बतौर मजदूरी कार्य के लिए प्रतिपक्षी के मुंशी के पास जिस दिन भी वह कार्य करता है, उसकी समुचित राशि प्रतिपक्षी के 
मुंशी द्वारा उसी दिन सांयकालीन भुगतान कर दी जाती है, प्रार्थी की किसी प्रकार से कोई भी राशि प्रतिपक्षी पर देय नहीं बनती है और न ही 
प्रतिपक्षी द्वारा प्रार्थी को माईन्स पर कार्य करने हेतु नियोजित किया गया, न ही दि. 25.03.2000 से 05.07.2001 तक प्रार्थी द्वारा कार्य किया है, 
इसलिए प्रतिपक्षी से प्रार्थी किसी भी प्रकार से कोई भी राशि को प्राप्त करने का अधिकारी नहीं है और प्रार्थना की है कि प्रार्थी का क्लेम प्रार्थना पत्र 
सव्यय खारिज किया जावे। 

4-साक्ष्य में प्रार्थी की ओर से स्वयं व अप्रार्थी की ओर से ओम सिंह के शपथ-पत्र प्रस्तुत हुए जिनसे परस्पर जिरह की गयी। प्रार्थी की ओर से 
दस्तावेजी साक्ष्य भी प्रस्तुत की गयी जिसका यथासमय उल्लेख किया जावेगा। 


5-उभयपक्ष की बहस अंतिम सुनी गईं जो की उनके द्वारा प्रस्तुत न्यायिक दृष्टांतों के अनुरूप ही रही है। 


6-हस्तगत प्रकरण में न्यायालय को यह देखना है कि क्या प्रार्थी श्रमिक को अप्रार्थी दिनांक 25.03.2000 से 05.07.2001 तक की कार्य अवधि के शेष 
वेतन 44000 /-रू. का भुगतान नहीं किया जाना उचित एवं वैध है अथवा नहीं? 


7-इस प्रकार उपरोक्त साक्ष्य के विवेचन से यह प्रकट हो रहा है कि प्रार्थी द्वारा 25.03.2000 से 05.07.2001 तक निरंतर अप्रार्थी के नियोजन में कार्य 
करने एवं अप्रार्थी द्वारा उक्त अवधि के बकाया वेतन 44000 /-रू. का भुगतान नहीं करने का कथन किया गया है। इसके विपरीत अप्रार्थी की ओर 
से प्रार्थी के उक्त कथनों का खण्डन किया गया है और यह स्पष्ट किया गया है कि प्रार्थी उनके यहां नियमित तौर पर कोई कार्य नहीं करता था 
बल्कि बतौर मजदूरी कार्य के लिए प्रतिपक्षी के मुंशी के पास जिस दिन भी कार्य करता, उसकी समुचित राशि प्रतिपक्षी के मुंशी द्वारा उसी दिन दे दी 
जाती थी एवं प्रार्थी ने उनके यहां दिनांक 25.03.2000 से 05.07.20001 तक कार्य नहीं किया है। हस्तगत प्रकरण में प्रार्थी द्वारा जो साक्ष्य पेश की गई 
है उसमें प्रदर्श डब्ल्यू 4 एवं Say 2 प्रार्थी को अप्रार्थी द्वारा भुगतान करने का रिकॉर्ड, प्रदर्श डब्ल्यू 3 Wel Hl सहायक श्रम आयुक्त के समक्ष पेश

3750 /-रू. होना भी विवादग्रस्त हो जाता है। वांछित अनुतोष प्राप्त करने के लिए प्रार्थी श्रमिक को अपनी साक्ष्य से यह सिद्ध करना आवश्यक है कि 
प्रार्थी ने उसके द्वारा वर्णित कार्य अवधि दि. 25.03.2000 से 05.07.20001 तक अप्रार्थी के नियोजन में कार्य किया हो एवं उसका मासिक वेतन 
3750 /-रू. हो, किन्तु प्रार्थी द्वारा प्रस्तुत उपरोक्त साक्ष्य से यह सिद्ध नहीं होता है कि प्रार्थी ने दि. 25.03.2000 से 05.07.2001 तक अप्रार्थी के 
नियोजन में कब से कब तक कार्य किया है| प्रकरण में प्रार्थी की ओर से हाजरी रजिस्टर, मस्टरोल, पे-स्लिप/वेतन पर्ची इत्यादि दस्तावेज भी 
प्रस्तुत नहीं किए गए S| प्रार्थी अमिक पर काम करने के तथ्य को साबित करने का जो भार है, उसमें प्रार्थी पक्ष द्वारा केवल मात्र अपने शपथ-पत्र में 
यह लिख देना कि प्रार्थी श्रमिक ने दि. 25.03.2000 से 05.07.2001 तक लगातार काम किया एवं उसका वेतन अप्रार्थी के नियोजन में 3750 / रू. 
मासिक हो, पर्याप्त नहीं होगा, अपितु उन्हें किसी ठोस मौखिक एवं प्रलेखीय / दस्तावेजी साक्ष्य से इस तथ्य को साबित करना होगा जिससे कि यह 
तथ्य सम्पुष्ट हो सके। परन्तु हस्तगत प्रकरण में पत्रावली पर उपलब्ध साक्ष्य से प्रार्थी अमिक यह तथ्य साबित करने में पूर्णतया असफल रहा है कि 
उसके द्वारा बताई गई कार्य अवधि दि. 25.03.2000 से 05.07.2001 तक कब से कब तक कितने कार्य किया है एवं उसका वेतन 3750 /-रू. प्रतिमाह 
के हिसाब से 44,000,//-रू. वेतन बकाया हो। इसके विपरीत पत्रावली उपलब्ध साक्ष्य प्रदर्श डब्ल्यू 1 व डब्ल्यू 2 एवं प्रार्थी के जिरह के दौरान किए 
गए कथनों से अप्रार्थी पक्ष के कथन अधिक विश्वसनीय प्रतीत होते हैं कि प्रार्थी ने जितने दिन अप्रार्थी के यहां कार्य किया है उसका भुगतान अप्रार्थी 
द्वारा उसी समय प्रार्थी को कर दिया गया है। ऐसे में प्रार्थी श्रमिक, अप्रार्थी से कोई अनुतोष प्राप्त करने का अधिकारी घोषित होने योग्य नहीं है एवं 
रेफ्रेंस भी इसी अनुरूप उत्तरित किया जाता है। 


8-परिणामतः श्रम मंत्रालय, भारत सरकार द्वारा अपनी प्रासांगिक अधिसूचना दि. 25.04.2011 के जरिये सम्प्रेषित निर्देश /विवाद को इसी अनुरूप 
उत्तरित किया जाता है कि प्रार्थी अमिक कैलाश यह सिद्ध कर पाने में असफल रहा है कि प्रार्थी, अप्रार्थी ओमसिंह राजावत पुत्र श्री 


6022 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA :SEPTEMBER 13, 2025/BHADRA 22, 1947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


कल्याण सिंह, माईन्स ऑनर सकतपुरा, कोटा के नियोजन में दि. 25.03.2000 से 05.07.2001 तक निरंतर अथवा कब से कब 
तक कार्यरत रहा है एवं कार्य करने के उपरांत प्रार्थी का अप्रार्थी के यहां 44,000 /-रू. वेतन शेष हो, इसलिए प्रार्थी श्रमिक, 
अप्रार्थी से किसी प्रकार का अनुतोष प्राप्त करने का अधिकारी नहीं है।

मेसर्स सुकेत लाइम स्टोन के प्रबंधतंत्र के संबद्ध नियोजकों और श्री गोरधन लाल, पत्थर खान कामगार यूनियन के बीच

68/2014)) को जैसा कि अनुलग्नक में दिखाया गया है, प्रकाशित करती है जो केन्द्रीय सरकार को सॉफ्ट कॉपी के साथ 
08.09.2025 को प्राप्त हुआ था |

भारत सरकार, श्रम मंत्रालय, नई दिल्‍ली के प्रासांगिक आदेश दिनांक 16.07.2007 के जरिये निर्देश विवाद, औद्योगिक विवाद अधिनियम, 
1947 (जिसे आगे “अधिनियम” से सम्बोधित किया जावेगा) की धारा 10(1)(घ) के अन्तर्गत इस न्‍्यायाधिकरण को अधिनिर्णयार्थ सम्प्रेषित किया गया 
a 


"Whether the action of the management of Sh. Attiquer Rehman S/o. Sh. Hazi Abdul Gafur, Lime Store 
Mines, Owner Pipakhedi, Ramganjmandi distt. Kota in terminating the service of Sh. Gordhan Lal ex Munshi, w.e-f. 
01-02-2006 and denying the payment of wage from June, 2005 to 31-01-2006 is legal and just ? If not what relief the 
concerned workman, shri Gordhan Lal is entitled to ?" 


2-उक्‍्त विवाद के न्यायालय में रेफर होने पर पंजीबद्ध कर पक्षकारों को उपस्थिति बाबत नोटिस जारी किए गए। नोटिस की पालना में प्रार्थी श्रमिक 
द्वारा उपस्थित होकर अपना संशोधित स्टेटमेन्ट ऑफ क्लेम न्यायालय के समक्ष प्रस्तुत कर संक्षिप्त: यह कथन किया गया है कि उसे प्रतिपक्षी 
नियोजक ने अपने यहां मुंशी के रिक्त पद पर दि. 01.121984 सेवा में नियोजित किया था। नियोजक ने श्रमिक को जून 2005 से वेतन का भुगतान 
नहीं किया गया, वेतन की मांग करता था तो उसे बाद में भुगतान करने की बात कहीं जाती रही। जनवरी 2006 में श्रमिक ने चढ़े हुए वेतन का 
भुगतान करने बाबत तकाजा किया और निवेदन किया कि उसको उसके परिवार का पालन पोषण करने में बहुत कठिनाइयां आ रही है साथ ही 
श्रमिक को दिनांक 01.02.2006 से ड्यूटी पर लेने से इन्कार कर दिया। श्रमिक दिनांक 01.02.2006 से नियोजक के यहां निरन्तर ड्यूटी हेतु प्रयास 
कर रहा है तथा वेतन भुगतान का तकाजा कर रहा है परंतु न तो चढ़े हुए वेतन का भुगतान किया और न श्रमिक को ड्यूटी पर लिया, इस बाबत 
श्रमिक ने दिनांक 22.05.2006 को रजिस्टर्ड डाक से पत्र प्रेषित किया जिसका कोई जवाब नहीं दिया गया ना ही ड्यूटी पर लिया ना ही भुगतान 
किया। नियोजक ने श्रमिक को दिनांक 01.02.2006 से अवैध प्रकार से नौकरी से हटा दिया है, जो कि छंटनी की परिमाषा में आता है, नौकरी से 
निकाले जाने से पूर्व ना तो एक माह का नोटिस दिया ना ही नोटिस वेतन दिया ना ऑफर किया और इस प्रकार अधिनियम की धारा 25 एफ का 
उल्लंघन किया गया है। श्रमिक ने नियोजक के यहां पर दिनांक 01.12.1984 से 31.01.2006 तक निरन्तर कार्य करके 240 दिन से अधिक समय तक 
कार्य किया है। अंत में प्रार्थना की गई कि श्रमिक को नियाजक से संपूर्ण पिछले वेतन सहित सेवा में बहाल किये जाने तथा जून 2005 से 31.01. 
2006 तक के चढ़े हुए वेतन का भुगतान मय ब्याज से कराया जावे। 


3-अप्रार्थी नियोजक की ओर से स्टेटमेन्ट ऑफ क्लेम का जवाब प्रस्तुत कर यह प्रतिवाद किया गया है कि दिनांक 01.02.2006 से प्रार्थी ने स्वयं ही 
बगैर किसी प्रकार की सूचना दिये तथा कारण बताये ड्यूटी पर आना बंद कर दिया तब अप्रार्थी द्वारा दिनांक 19.05.2006 को एक नोटिस अपने 
अधिवक्ता के माध्यम से प्रार्थी को प्रेषित किया जो उसे प्राप्त हो गया इस नोटिस के द्वारा ही अप्रार्थी द्वारा प्रार्थी की सेवाएँ समाप्त की गई थी इससे 
पूर्व सेवा समाप्ति के संबंध में कोई कार्यवाही नहीं की गई जो कार्यवाही सेवा समाप्ति की करी गई उसकी प्रार्थी को पूर्ण जानकारी है तथा उक्त 
नोटिस के माध्यम से ही उसकी बकाया वेतन को प्राप्त कर लेने के संबंध में सूचना प्रेषित की गयी थी परंतु श्रमिक कार्यालय में उपस्थित नहीं हुआ 
जबकि अप्रार्थी सदैव ही प्रार्थी की बकाया राशि अदा करने के लिए तैयार व तत्पर रहा है जबकि प्रार्थी अपने बकाया वेतन का भुगतान प्राप्त करने 
को तैयार नहीं है। प्रार्थी द्वारा स्वयं ही अप्रार्थी के यहां कार्य पर आना बंद कर दिया था जिस कारण से प्रार्थी की सेवाएँ नोटिस के माध्यम से समाप्त 
की गई है क्योंकि प्रार्थी द्वारा ड्यूटी पर ना आने के संबंध में अप्रार्थी के यहां कोई सूचना नहीं दी गई थी ना ही इसके संबंध में कोई कारण बतलाया 
गया था। अंत में Wet or स्टेटमेंट ऑफ क्लेम सव्यय खारिज किये जाने का निवेदन किया। 


4-साक्ष्य में प्रा्थी की ओर से स्वयं का शपथ पत्र प्रस्तुत किया गया जिससे अप्रार्थी अधिवक्ता ने जिरह की गई, अप्रार्थी की ओर से बावजूद अवसर 
साक्ष्य प्रस्तुत करना नहीं चाहा जिस पर अप्रार्थी साक्ष्य बंद की गयी। प्रार्थी की ओर से दस्तावेजी साक्ष्य भी प्रस्तुत की गई है जिसका यथासमय 
उल्लेख किया जावेगा 


5-उभयपक्ष की बहस सुनी गई जो उनकी ओर से प्रस्तुत अपने-अपने अभ्यावेदनों के अनुरूप ही रही है प्रार्थी प्रतिनिधि का मुख्य रूप से यही तर्क 
रहा है कि अप्रार्थी द्वारा उसे दिनांक 01.02.2006 से ड्यूटी पर लेने से इन्कार कर दिया sie weit द्वारा sell नियाजक के यहां दिनांक 01.12.1984 
से उसकी सेवा समाप्ति से पूर्व 200 दिवस से अधिक अवधि तक कार्य किया है और अप्रार्थी की ओर से प्रार्थी को दिनांक 19.05.2006 को कोई 
नोटिस ड्यूटी पर आने के लिए तथा बकाया वेतन के लिए नोटिस प्रेषित नहीं किया गया है।

नियोजित होने का कथन किया है, जिसके संबंध में जवाब क्लेम में अप्रार्थी द्वारा उक्त कथनों को स्वीकार करते हुए अप्रार्थी के नियेजन में प्रार्थी की 
सेवा दिनांक 01.12.1984 से प्रारम्भ होना स्वीकार की गयी है। इसके अतिरिक्त प्रार्थी की ओर से स्टेटमेंट ऑफ ala के पेरा संख्या 2 में जून 2005 
से जनवरी 2006 तक का वेतन नहीं दिया जाना तथा अप्रार्थी द्वारा दिनांक 01.02.2006 से ड्यूटी पर लेने से मना करने का soa है जिसके जवाब 
में अप्रार्थी द्वारा यह दिया गया है कि 1 जनवरी 2006 को प्रार्थी स्वयं नौकरी छोड़कर गया है और जिसके संबंध 4 19.05.2006 को एक नोटिस 
अधिवक्ता के माध्यम से प्रेषित करवाया गया है। हालाकि प्रार्थी द्वारा अपनी जिरह में अप्रार्थी के नियोजन में सेवा पृथकता दिनांक से पूर्व 240 दिवस 
तक कार्य करने का कोई दस्तावेज प्रस्तुत नहीं करने का कथन किया है परंतु उक्त जवाब से यह स्पष्ट है कि प्रार्थी ने अप्रार्थी के नियोजन में बतौर 
क्लर्क दिनांक 01.12.1984 से कार्य करना आरम्भ किया जो कि दिनांक 01.02.2006 को बंद किया, जिससे यह तथ्य प्रमाणित रहता है कि प्रार्थी ने 
उसकी सेवा पृथकता दिनांक 01.02.2006 से पूर्व अप्रार्थी के नियोजन में 12 कैलेण्डर माहों में 220 दिवस से अधिक अवधि तक कार्य किया है और 
उक्त तथ्य अप्रार्थी के स्वयं के द्वारा दिये गये जवाब के अभिवचनों से साबित हो रहा है, जिसके प्रमाणन के लिये पृथक से किसी प्रकार की 
दस्तोवजी साक्ष्य की आवश्यकता नहीं रहती है। ऐसे में प्रार्थी की ओर से प्रस्तुत दस्तावेजी व मौखिक साक्ष्य से प्रार्थी का उसकी सेवा पृथकता दिनांक 
01.02.200 से पूर्व दिनांक 01.02.2005 से 31.01.2006 तक की अवधि में 12 कैलेण्डर माहों में अप्रार्थी के नियोजन में 240 दिन तक लगातार कार्य 
करना प्रमाणित होता है, लिहाजा अप्रार्थी द्वारा अधिनियम के आज्ञापक प्रावधानों की अवहेलना कर प्रार्थी को हटाया जाना छंटनी की परिभाषा में आता 
है जो अनुचित व अवैध होने से प्रार्थी का स्टेटमेंट ऑफ ala स्वीकार करने योग्य पाया जाता है। 
इसके अतिरिक्त प्रार्थी द्वारा दिनांक 02.06.2006 को समझोता अधिकारी के समक्ष प्रतिवेदन प्रस्तुत किया गया जिसका प्रतिउत्तर अप्रार्थी 
द्वारा अपने क्लेम के जवाब अनुरूप प्रस्तुत कर कथन किया गया कि उसने जरिये अधिवक्ता दिनांक 19.05.2006 के प्रार्थी को नोटिस प्रेषित किया 
गया और ड्यूटी पर वापस आने तथा अपना हिसाब करने का अंकन किया गया। जब हम पत्रावली का समग्र रूप से अवलोकन करते हैं तो ऐसा 
कोई पत्र जो दिनांक 19.05.2006 को जारी किया गया हो और प्रार्थी को प्राप्त हुआ हो उपलब्ध नहीं पाया जाता है, इसके अतिरिक्त जब प्रार्थी द्वारा 
उक्त दिनांक 19.05.2006 को जारी पत्र अप्रार्थी से तलब करवाये जाने का प्रार्थना पत्र पेश किया तो अप्रार्थी द्वारा उक्त प्रार्थना पत्र के जवाब में स्पष्ट 
रूप से उक्त दिनांक 19.05.2006 का नोटिस जो जरिये अधिवक्ता उसके द्वारा प्रार्थी को प्रेषित किया गया है, उसके पावर एण्ड पजेशन में नहीं होने 
का कथन किया गया है, ऐसे में यह प्रमाणित नहीं रहता है कि प्रार्थी द्वारा दिनांक 19.05.2006 को ऐसा कोई पत्र प्रार्थी को ड्यूटी पर वापस आने 
तथा अपना हिसाब का पैसा लेने बाबत प्रेषित किया गया हो जबकि दूसरी तरह प्रार्थी की ओर से ड्यूटी पर लिये जाने तथा उसका बकाया वेतन 
भुगतान करने बाबत पत्र जरिये रजिस्टर्ड डाक से अप्रार्थी को प्रेषित किये जाने का कथन किया है जिसके संबंध में दस्तावेजी साक्ष्य के रूप में प्रदर्श 
2 लगायत 3 प्रस्तुत कर प्रदर्शित भी करवाये है जो उसके gery व शपथ पत्र में अंकि तथ्यों की पूर्णरूपेण ताईद करते हैं। ऐसे में अप्रार्थी द्वारा प्रार्थी 
को दिनांक 01.02.2006 को नौकरी से हटाये जाने का कृत्य अनफेयर लेबर प्रेक्टिस की परिभाषा में आता है क्योंकि अप्रार्थी द्वारा मूल रूप से 
यही कथन किया गया है कि प्रार्थी ea नौकरी का परित्याग करके गया है और जिसको बुलाने का पत्र प्रार्थी को अप्रार्थी द्वारा प्रेषित किया गया है, 
परंतु ऐसा कोई पत्र अप्रार्थी साक्ष्य में प्रस्तुत नहीं कर सका है, जो कि उपरोक्त विवेचन से प्रमाणित नहीं रहता है। 
8-अब जहाँ तक प्रार्थी के अनुतोष का प्रश्न है, न्यायालय ने अप्रार्थी नियोजक द्वारा अधिनियम के आज्ञापक प्रावधानों का उल्लंघन करके प्रार्थी के 
सेवा पृथककरण को अवैध मान लिया गया है। अब चूंकि हस्तगत प्रकरण में प्रार्थी ने अपने दिनांक 27.05.2025 को लेखबद्ध बयानों में अपनी उम्र 70 
वर्ष बताई है जोकि निश्चित रूप से अप्रार्थी के नियमानुसार अधिवार्षिकी आयु को प्राप्त कर चुका है एवं well ar सेवा पृथकता से लगभग 19 वर्ष हो 
चुके है किन्तु इसके साथ ही न्यायालय के ध्यानान्तर्गत यह तथ्य भी महत्वपूर्ण है कि प्रार्थी ने अपने प्रकरण सम्बन्धी एक लम्बी कानूनी लड़ाई लड़ी है 
इस दौरान उसको आर्थिक व मानसिक वेदना भी झेलनी पड़ी है। अतः प्रकरण के समस्त तथ्य, परिस्थितियों व Nagar Parishad, Kota at

लाभों के अनुतोष के स्थान पर क्षतिपूर्ति स्वरूप एक मुश्त 2.50,000/-रू. (अक्षर दो लाख पचास रूपये) की राशि अप्रार्थी से दिलवाया जाना 
न्यायोचित प्रतीत होता है साथ ही अप्रार्थी द्वारा समझौता अधिकारी के समक्ष प्रस्तुत जवाब में भी प्रार्थी को जून 2005 से 31.01.2006 तक के वेतन 
भुगतान को अदा करने हेतु नोटिस दिनांक 19.05.2006 प्रेषित कर तैयार व तत्पर होना अभिकथित किया है और यह कथन किया है कि प्रार्थी ही 
उसे प्राप्त करने अप्रार्थी के पास नही आया है जबकि उक्त दिनांक 19.05.2006 को नोटिस जारी नहीं किया जाना उपरोक्त विवेचन से प्रमाणित रहता 
है, ऐसे में प्रार्थी जून 2005 से 31.01.2006 तक का वेतन भी अप्रार्थी से प्राप्त करने का अधिकारी है और प्राप्त tora भी इसी अनुरूप उत्तरित होने 
योग्य है।

मालिक, पीपाखेड़ी, रामगंजमण्डी, जिला कोटा द्वारा दिनांक 01.02.2006 से सेवा से पृथक करना उचित एवं वैध नहीं है। अतः प्रार्थी अमिक समस्त 
आनुषंगिक लाभों के अनुतोष के स्थान पर क्षतिपूर्ति स्वरूप एक ART रूपये 2.50,000,/-(अक्षरे दो लाख पचास रूपये) की राशि अप्रार्थी नियोजक से

AWARD. 


In exercise of powers conferred under clause (d) of sub-section (1) and sub —section (2A) of Section 10 of 
the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947),Government Of India through the Ministry of Labour, vide its Order 
No.L-2601 1/3 1/2018-IR(M) dated 07/01/2019 has been pleased to refer the following dispute between the employer 
1.6. management of M/s Bokaro Steel Plant of SAIL and other and their workman through General Secretary, 
Jharkhand Krantikari Mazdoor Union, B.S. City, Bokaro for adjudication by this Tribunal: 


“Whether the action of the management of M/s Manoj Enterprises for retrenchment of 45 contract 
workers (Annexure-A) engaged in the Stores Department of Bokaro Steel Plant of SAIL, Bokaro 
without notice/payment of notice pay and retrenchment compensation and denial to reinstate contract 
workers, is proper and justified? If not, to what relief the concerned workers are entitled to?

2. The case of the workman is that the union raised an Industrial Dispute vide its statement of demand dated 


29.11.2016 for which the issues pertaining to the unlawful retrenchment of 45 contract workers working in Stores 
Department of SAIL, Bokaro Steel Plant were raised before the Asstt. Labour Commissioner(Central) & Conciliation 
Officer, Dhanbad and on receipt of the same the Conciliation Officer took up the issue with the General Manager 
(P&A) SAIL, Bokaro Steel Plant vide letter dated 26.12.2016 whereby the management was asked to furnish their 
comments in respect of letter dated 29.11.2016 and at the same time some information was sought from the union as 
well for holding conciliation proceedings on the date fixed but even after several sittings the settlement could not be 
arrived accordingly the Conciliation Officer sent a Conciliation failure report u/s 12(4) of the I.D. Act to the Ministry 
of Labour & Employment, Govt. of India on 31.10.2018 and thereafter the Appropriate Govt. vide 


6030 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA :SEPTEMBER 13, 2025/BHADRA 22, 1947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


No. L-26011/31/2018-IR(M) dated 07.01.2019 referred the dispute to Central Govt. Industrial Tribunal cum Labour 
Court No.1, Dhanbad for adjudication with the following schedule:- 


“Whether the action of the management of M/s Manoj Enterprises for retrenchment of 45 contract workers 
(Annexure-A) engaged in the Stores Department of Bokaro Steel Plant of SAIL, Bokaro without notice/payment of 
notice pay and retrenchment compensation and denial to reinstate contract workers, is proper and justified? If not, to 
what relief the concerned workers are entitled to?” 


It is further case of the workman that all the 45 said contract workers continued to work under the contractor 
M/s. Manoj Enterprises from the year 2004 to 2010 and thereafter they were deployed with M/s. Anil Kumar, a 
contractor from the period April 2010 to 2013 carrying out the same job on the same place of work. The contract 
workers got engaged again with M/s. Manoj Enterprises from the year 2013 till their services were dispensed with the 
contractor while the validity of work order was till 17.08.2016. The services of the listed workmen were terminated 
even when their Gate Passes were valid and before termination no notice was served to them by the contractor 
violating Section 25(N) & 25(F) of the Industrial Dispute Act, 1947 which otherwise led to illegal retrenchment. In 
addition to the above, M/s Manoj Enterprises was also to comply the provisions contained under section 25(N)(1) (a), 
(0), (c) and 25(P) of the Industrial Disputes Act 1947. 


3. On the other hand the management filed W/S cum rejoinder on 27.11.2019 submitting that the 
present reference is not maintainable and the management of Bokaro Steel Plant never appointed the workmen 
concerned, hence question of termination of their services by Bokaro Steel Plant does not arises. There is no 
employer-employee relationship exists between the parties. The job contracts are awarded to contractors for carrying 
out jobs of non-core nature at various departments of BSL, Bokaro through tendering process and the contractor who 
has been awarded contract used to deploy their workmen according to the requirement for execution of the job 
awarded to them and the workmen employed by them used to work under direct supervision of the contractor and 
therefore the contractors concerned are liable to payment of wages to their workmen. 


4. On perusal of the record it further transpires that a reply dated 27.11.19 has also been filed on behalf of Manoj 
Enterprises submitting therein that M/s. Manoj Enterprises is a registered contractor of SAIL, Bokaro Steel Plant, and 
they are associated with Stores Deptt. of SAIL, Bokaro Steel Plant through various loading and unloading of materials 
from one point to another as per the requirement of department and they engaged the contract workers under the 
purview of The Contract Labour (regulation & Abolition) Act, 1970 and they are bound to pay the statutory dues as 
per the provisions under said Act and the Factories Act, 1948. These contract workers are governed by the Contract 
Labour (regulation & Abolition) Act 1970 and they do not fall within the purview of Section 25F of Industrial 
Dispute Act, 1947. The workers who have left the organization without information and kept the Gate Passes of the 
SAIL, Bokaro Steel Plant with them only, Manoj Enterprises is not in a position to pay the notice pay or retrenchment 
benefit as they had left as per their choice and willingness and they also did not inform either in writing or in verbal. 
Those workers have already been paid their earned leave wages and bonus in their individual account. As per the FOC 
report M/s. Manoj Enterprises has no objection to pay the notice pay as per the norms and practice in the industry to 
only 16 persons whose names are notified in the notice dated 11.02.2016 but they cannot pay anything to the workers 
who left the organization without information other than earned leave and bonus that they already paid. It has further 
been submitted that in the period of 2010 to 2014, M/s. Manoj Enterprises did not work continuously as the work 
order issued was for different period and after completion of the work order the entire salary and other statutory dues 
as per the applicability has already been paid to the concerned workers. From April 2010 to 2013, the workers were 
engaged with some M/s. Anil Kumar, for which they have no idea or knowledge. As on date M/s. Manoj Enterprises 
has no objection to pay the 16 workers as per notice dated 11.02.2016 and any of the work order the cost of 
retrenchment of notice period is not included as the work order is being issued for the fixed term and the employed 
person is aware of their job as per validity of the Gate Passes. The work order is issued on the JOB CONTRACT 
basis or tonnage basis directly linked with plant production. 


2: It further transpires that a settlement was arrived at between the parties that was prepared in black and white 
and has been submitted before this Tribunal in FORM-H which has been placed on record on 23.02.2022 and hearing 
was made on the same on 10.07.2025 in the presence of the parties concerned. The recital of the settlement is as 
under:- 


(A) The contractor Manoj Enterprises agreed to pay the notice period for 15 contract workers out of listed 
45 whose names are mentioned below at the rate of wages as per skill applicable in 2016. 


(B) Regarding re-employment of left over 30 contract workers, M/s. Manoj Enterprises agreed to provide 
employment as and when required as per skills activity in work order received from SAIL, BSL only in future if any. 
If the employment is available in future the requirement will be fresh request to fulfillment of all pre-employment 
editions as per norms of the Principal employer. 


The other party, General Secretary of, JMKU, Bokaro consented in writing for No Objection. It further 
transpires that both the parties put their respective signatures below the written settlkement and two witnesses have 
also signed the same. 


[भाग ए---खण्ड 361)] भारत का राजपत्र : सितम्बर 13, 2025/ भाद्र 22, 1947 6031 


6. Now, as the issues have been settled between the parties and written settlement under Section 2(p) 
of the Industrial Dispute Act, 1947 is available on record, this case is hereby allowed in terms of settlement between 
the parties and the written settlement shall form part of the award. The case is disposed of. Consign the record in the 
record room. Let the copies of Award in duplicate be sent to the Ministry of Labour & Employment, Government of 
India, New Delhi for information and notification. 


SACHINDRA KUMAR PANDEY, Presiding Officer 
नई दिल्‍ली, 8 सितम्बर, 2025

मेसर्स बोकारो स्टील प्लांट, सेल के प्रबंधतंत्र के संबद्ध नियोजकों और उनके कर्मचारी के बीच अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट केन्द्रीय 
सरकार औद्योगिक अधिकरण एवं श्रम न्‍्यायालय-1, धनबाद, Tate (रिफरेन्स न.-38/2012) को जैसा कि अनुलग्नक में 
दिखाया गया है, प्रकाशित करती है जो केन्द्रीय सरकार को सॉफ्ट कॉपी के साथ 08.09.2025 को प्राप्त हुआ था |

New Delhi, the 8th September, 2025 


S.O. 1628.—In pursuance of Section 17 of the Industrial Dispute Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central 
Government hereby publishes the award (Reference No. 38/2012) of the Central Government Industrial Tribunal 
cum Labour Court-1, Dhanbad as shown in the Annexure, in the Industrial dispute between the employers in 
relation to M/s Bokaro Steel Plant, SAIL and Their workman which was received along with soft copy of the 
award by the Central Government on 08.09.2025. 


[No. L-29012/11/2012-IR(M)] 
SALONI, Dy. Director 
ANNEXURE 
BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL NO.1,DHANBAD 
In the matter of reference U/S 10 (1) (9) & (2A) of I.D.Act. 1947. 
Reference Case No. 38/2012 


Employer in relation to the management of M/s. Bokaro Steel Plant, SAIL, Bokaro. 
AND. 
Their workman. 


Present: Shri Sachindra Kumar Pandey 


Presiding Officer 
Appearances: 
For the Employer :- Sri D.K. Verma, Ld. Advocate. 
For the workman. :- Sri C.P. Agrawal (in person) 
State : Jharkhand. Industry:-Steel Plant 
Dated 28/07/2025 
AWARD. 


In exercise of powers conferred under clause (d) of sub-section (1) and sub —section (2A) of Section 10 of 
the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947),Government Of India through the Ministry of Labour, vide its Order 
No.L-29012/11/2012-(IR(M)) dated 04/06/2012, has been pleased to refer the following dispute between the 
employer i.e. management of M/s Bokaro Steel Plant of SAIL and their workman through General Secretary, Bokaro 
Karmachari Panchayat, B.S. City, Bokaro for adjudication by this Tribunal: 


“Whether the action of the management of Bokaro Steel Plant, SAIL in deducting penal rent for 
occupation of Qr. No. VIIIA-2255 w.e.f. Dec. 1986 to October 1990 from the wages of 


6032 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA :SEPTEMBER 13, 2025/BHADRA 22, 1947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


Shri C.P. Agarwal, Staff No. 075855 is proper and justified? And whether the action of the 
management of Bokaro Steel Plan, SAIL in allegedly wrong fixing the scales of the said workman from 
21/6/1979 till the date of VRS is proper and justified? What relief the workman is entitled to?” 


2. The case of the applicant/workman in short as per his WS is that he was appointed on 24.11.1970 and one 
residential Qr. No. VII-A 2255 D type was allotted to him and he occupied the same and started residing therein 
along with his family members but the management illegally started making deduction of penal rent from the wages 
of workman @ Rs. 477/- per month w.e.f. June 1986 without consent of workman or without any reason. The 
workman protested against the same but even thereafter the deduction continued and then he filed a case under 
Section 15(2) and (3) of the payment of wages Act before the State Labour Court, B.S. City which was awarded in 
favour of workman and the management was directed to refund the amount deducted from the wages of the workman. 
But inspite of that the management continued to deduct penal rent per month till Oct.1990 total Rs.21996/-His appeal 
against the said order was also dismissed. He also filed another case under Section 33A of the 1.10. Act, 1947 before 
the State Labour Court, Bokaro raising allegations of change of service conditions by way of denial of promotion, 
supersession by juniors etc. which was numbered as Misc. Case No. 10/1986 and the Labour Court, Bokaro passed 
award in favour of the workman granting promotion to construction supervisor Gr-1 from 6.06.77, additional 
divisional engineer from 21.06.79, divisional engineer from 17.02.83 and zonal engineer from30.06.87 with all 
consequential benefits. The management filed a writ petition against that order being CWJC No. 1485 of 1991 before 
the Hon’ble Patna High Court, Ranchi Bench and prayed to stay the operation of the award but he was refused and 
thereafter the management implemented the award and he was given promotion but illegally reduced the basic wages 
from Rs.881/- to Rs.850/-per month causing the workman heavy loss. As per the award, the basic wages should be 
Rs.950/-per month in E-1 as on 21.06.79. The workman concerned retired on VRS on 31.03.2004 and the 
management paid him less compensation under the VRS. He is entitled to receive total Rs. 2,28,524.82/-as well as 
refund of Rs.21969/-with interest from the management. 


3. On the other hand the case of the management as per the written statement dated 18.01.2013 is that the present 
reference is not maintainable. The concerned workman is not a ‘workman’ under Section 2(s) of the I.D. Act, 1947. 
The concerned workman is holding the post of Executive and performing managerial job, as such, the present dispute 
is not maintainable. He is not covered under the provision of I.D. Act, 1947. The applicant C.P Agarwal also 
submitted V.R.S as per the Scheme of the management which was accepted and he had received all the benefits under 
the V.R.S Scheme as such no dispute can be raised by the concerned workman after the acceptance of the benefits. 
The workman was originally appointed as Construction Supervisor w.e.f. 24.11.1970. During the tenure of his service 
Sri C.P. Agarwal filed an application u/s 33A of the I.D. Act claiming promotion on different grades. Under Section 
33A of the I.D. Act workman can file an application if his service is changed during the pendency of the reference 
case in which he is involved/connected and the Labour Court or Tribunal has no power to pass order/award for 
promotion of a workman. Surprisingly enough the Labour Court Bokaro passed an order in Misc.case No. 10/86 filed 
under Section 33A of the I.D. Act by Sri C.P. Agarwal wherein and whereby directed the management to promote the 
applicant C.P. Agarwal to the post of Construction Supervisor Grade-I w.e.f. 06.06.1977, Addl. Divisional Engineer 
w.e.f. 21.6.1979, Divisional Engineer w.e.f. 17.02.1983 and Zonal Engineer w.e.f. 30.06.1987. The illegal order of the 
Labour Court Bokaro was challenged before the Hon’ble High Court by the management in CWJC No. 1485/91. 
During the pendency of the writ petition the management implemented the award fully and Sri C.P. Agarwal was 
given all the benefits as per award including promotion and difference of wages. The Hon’ble High Court ultimately 
quashed the award/order of the Labour Court and then C.P. Agarwal filed SLP before the Hon’ble Supreme Court 
against that order but the Hon’ble Supreme Court dismissed the appeal of the applicant C.P Agarwal but taking into 
consideration of promoting the concerned workman during the pendency of the writ petition, the management was 
directed not to revert him to the lower post. The workman also received his wages and other benefits more than he 
was entitled but still he has filed the present case being emboldened by previous facts mentioned above. The 
workman is a litigant officer. He also filed M.J. Case No. 05/89 for refund of penal rent deducted by the management 
from December, 1986 to April, 1989 but the Labour court dismissed the same and then C.P. Agarwal preferred a writ 
petition being W.P.(L) No. 281/2003 which was dismissed by the Hon’ble High Court by order dated 06.11.2007. The 
same issue of deducting penal rent is again being raised. Sri C.P. Agarwal has accepted V.R.S. Scheme and he now 
retired from service and received all his benefits and therefore no claim can be preferred by him after receiving the 
same and the action of the management of deducting penal rent for occupying the quarter illegally is legal, proper and 
justified. The wages of the workman was never wrongly fixed from 21.06.1979 till the date of his V.R.S. The award 
of the Labour Court was quashed by the Hon’ble High Court and at last it was prayed to dismiss the reference case of 
the applicant. 


A rejoinder dated 18/01/2013 was also filed to the w/s of the management in which the workman has denied all 
the claims and statement of the management submitting that the deduction of penal rent was illegal and his pay was 
wrongly fixed after his promotion. 


4. In support of his case the workman namely Chandrika Prasad Agarwal has examined himself as WW-1. 


[MTT Il—ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : सितम्बर 13, 2025/ ATE 22, 1947 6033 


The workman has also got Exhibited the following documents in favour of his case:- 


Exhibit W-1- Xerox copy of order of P.W. Case No. 22 of 1986 of Labour Court, Bokaro Steel City 
dated 21.12.1988. 


Exhibit W-2- Xerox copy of Judgment of Misc. Appeal No. 65 of 1989 of the 1“ Addl. Disctrict Judge, 
Bokaro dated 13.04.2000. 


Exhibit W-3- Xerox copy of Award of Misc. Case No. 10 of 1986 of the Labour Court, Bokaro Steel 
City dated 17.01.1991. 


Exhibit W-4- Xerox copy of order of the High Court of Judicature at Patna, Ranchi Bench, Ranchi 
in CWJC No. 1485 of 1991(R) dated 23.09.1991. 


Exhibit W-5- Xerox copy of office order dated 17/18.02.1992 issued by Bokaro Steel Plant, Personnel 
Department Organisation Development Section regarding pay fixation of Sri C.P Agarwal after promotion. 


Exhibit W-6- Xerox copy of Allotment Order dated 23.03.1979 of Quarter No. VIII-A/2255 to C.P. 
Agarwal. 


Exhibit W-7- Xerox copy of Circular dated 16.07.1982 of Bokaro Steel Plant, Personnel Department 
(Recruitment Section) for filling up the post of Assistant Divisional Engineer. 


5. On the other hand the management in support of his case, has examined Ranodip Banerjee as MW-1. 
The Management has also got Exhibited the following documents in favour of his case. 

Exhibit M-1- Allotment Order dated 23.3.79. 
Exhibit M-2- Order passed in M.J. Case No. 5/89 by the Learned Labour Court, Bokaro. 
Exhibit M-3- Acceptance of VRS of the concerned workman, C.P. Agarwal. 
Exhibit M-4- Office Order dated 17/18.02.1992. 
Exhibit M-5- Hon’ble High Court’s Order dated 5.11.2007 passed in W.P. (L) No. 2881/2003. 
Exhibit M-6 —Photo copy of Office Order of Delegation of Powers of Bokaro Steel Plant 


6. Heard both sides and perused the evidences on record. 


7. The workman Sri C.P. Agarwal has submitted that he was appointed on 24.11.1970 and since then he was 
working sincerely. He was allotted a residential quarter by the management in which he started residing alongwith his 
family members but the management started illegally deducting penal rent from his wages @ Rs. 477/- per month 
w.e.f. June 1986 and thereafter he filed a case u/s 15(2) and (3) of the Payment of Wages Act for refund of the same 
that was allowed by the Labour Court, Bokaro Steel City but even thereafter the management continued to deduct the 
same. The management also filed an appeal against the said order but the same was dismissed by the Ld. A.D.J, 
Bokaro. The workman also filed a case u/s 33 A of the I.D. Act, 1947 before the Labour Court, Bokaro Steel City that 
was allowed in his favour and the management was directed for his promotion with all consequential benefits and full 
back wages. That order was challenged before the Hon’ble High Court but in the meantime he was promoted and 
Award was implemented by the management but the management illegally reduced the basic wages of the workman 
from Rs. 881/- to Rs. 850/- per month w.e.f. 21.06.1979 and due to which he suffered heavy financial loss. As per the 
award his basic wages should be Rs. 950/- per month in Grade E-I as on 21.06.1979. He is entitled to receive total Rs. 
2,28,524.82/-as well as refund of Rs.21969/-with interest from the management. 


8. On the other hand the Ld. Counsel of the management submitted that the present reference is not maintainable 
because the applicant C.P. Agarwal is not a ‘workman’ w/s 2(s) of the 1.0. Act, 1947. He was appointed as a 
Construction Supervisor and he was working at Managerial Post. He further submitted that the applicant’s pay has 
been legally fixed and penal rent was deducted because he had illegally occupied the quarter. After his taking VRS, he 
has been paid entire dues amount and nothing is outstanding to the management and prayed to dismiss the reference 
case. 


9. It is important to mention that this case was decided by this Tribunal vide Award dated 27.01.2017 in favour of the 
workman but when the award was challenged by the management before the Hon’ble Jharkhand High Court the 
award was set aside vide his Lordship’s order dated 06.05.2024 in W.P. (L) No. 2771 of 2017 and the case was 
remitted to this Tribunal again for passing fresh award and in Paragraph-16 of the Order, the Hon’ble Court has very 
clearly stated that whether the workman was a workman or not within the meaning of section 2(s) of the Industrial 
Dispute Act, 1947 was a jurisdictional issue which requires to be decided on the basis of materials available on 
record. Accordingly this case was remitted back before this Tribunal for passing fresh award. 


10. On hearing both sides and perusing the entire materials on record this Tribunal finds that there are three issues to 
be decided by this Tribunal which are as follows:- 


6034 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA :SEPTEMBER 13, 2025/BHADRA 22, 1947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


(i) Whether the applicant C.P. Agarwal is a workman u/s 2(s) of the Industrial Dispute Act, 1947? 


(ii) Whether the action of the management of Bokaro Steel Plant, SAIL in deducting penal rent for occupation of 
Qr. No. VHIA-2255 w.e.f. Dec. 1986 to October, 1990 from his wages is proper and justified? 


(iii) Whether the management of Bokaro Steel Plant, SAIL has wrongly fixed the scales of C.P. Agarwal from 
21.06.1979 till the date of VRS and if not what relief the workman is entitled to? 


11. As regards the first issue, as to whether the applicant C.P. Agarwal is a workman u/s 2(s) of the 1.10. Act, 
1947 or not, this is a jurisdictional issue and most important to be decided first. 


On this point the applicant has stated in his written statement that he was appointed on 24.11.1970 and 
since then he was working sincerely, honestly, efficiently without any blemish or spot all through out his service 
career till his retirement under VRS but he has stated nothing in the entire W/S regarding the nature of his work 1.6. 
what work he was doing during his service period. 


As regards the oral evidence, Sri C.P. Agarwal has examined himself as WW-1 and in his testimony he 
has deposed that he was appointed in the services of SAIL, Bokaro Steel Plant, Bokaro Steel City w.e.f. 24.11.1970 as 
Construction Supervisor Grade-III and the post of Construction Supervisor is a technical post for which the minimum 
qualification required was 3 years full time diploma in any branch of engineering which he possessed on the date of 
his entry in service and he was allocated the job of Technical/Engineering Work by the management and the channel 
of promotion was also on technical job line as per company’s seniority & Promotion Rules. He was doing the 
Technical Job on shifts in the plant and he was neither given any power to appoint/dismiss any workman of the plant 
nor he was given any power to demote any workman, sanction leave as also to take any disciplinary action against 
any workman or the junior staff till the date of his retirement. The power of delegation as has been filed by the 
management in this case is only a myth having no authoritative force and was not in existence and was never acted 
upon during the entire period of his service. He has further deposed that he was given promotion to the post of 
Construction Supervisor Grade-H/Grade-I and thereafter to the Grade E-1, E-2, E-3, E-4 on technical line and when 
he was not given promotion in accordance with seniority, he filed a case bearing no. 10/86 before the Ld. Labour 
Court, Bokaro Steel City u/s 33A of the LD. Act, 1947 and he was allowed promotion namely Construction 
Supervisor Grade-I w.e.f. 6.6.1977, Additional Divisional Engineer w.e.f. 21.06.1979, Divisional Engineer w.e.f. 
17.02.1982 and Zonal Engineer w.e.f. 30.06.1987. He has denied that at the time of his retirement he had full power 
to sanction advance for purchase of conveyance, to approve TA/LTC/LLTC/Medical Bills to sanction advance for 
construction/purchase of lands etc, sanction payment of gratuity, reimbursement of bills, to issue show cause 
notice/warning imposing minor punishment for non-executives working in the department under the management 
company. He further stated that he worked on technical job on shifts and it was neither managerial nor administrative 
or supervisory. 


In the cross-examination he has admitted that though he was appointed as Construction Supervisor, he 
was supervising and also working in construction works. 


12. On perusal of the pleadings and oral evidence of the applicant Sri C.P. Agarwal it is evident that he has admitted 
in his testimony that he was appointed as Construction Supervisor on 24.11.1970 and later on by the order of the 
Court he was promoted at the post of Construction Supervisor Grade-I w.e.f. 6.6.1977, Additional Divisional 
Engineer w.e.f. 21.6.1979, Divisional Engineer w.e.f. 17.2.1982 and Zonal Engineer w.e.f. 30.6.1987. 
As regards the documentary evidence, the applicant has not adduced any documentary evidence showing his actual 
work done by him. 


Thus the applicant has no where disclosed in his pleading or evidence as to what was the nature of his work 
and what work he was doing during his service period. Though he has submitted that he started his job on the post of 
Construction Supervisor and he was promoted to Zonal Engineer at the time of his VRS. 


13. On the other hand the management in his W/S has stated that C.P. Agarwal is not a workman u/s 2(s) of the 
I.D. Act 1947 as he was holding the post of executive and was performing managerial job. He was originally 
appointed as Construction Supervisor w.e.f. 24.11.1970. He has also admitted that by the order of the Labour court, 
CP Agrawal was promoted lastly up to the post of Zonal Engineer. 


The witness of the management that is MW-1, Ranodip Banerjee has stated in his testimony that Sri 
C.P. Agarwal was appointed as Construction Supervisor we.f. 24.11.1970 and he also admitted that the Labour Court, 
Bokaro Steel City in Misc. Case No. 10/86 gave direction to the management for promotion of C.P. Agarwal to the 
post of Constructor Supervisor Grade-I w.e.f. 6.6.1977, Additional Divisional Engineer w.e.f. 21.6.1979, Divisional 
Engineer w.e.f. 17.02.1982 and Zonal Engineer w.e.f. 30.6.1987. He has further deposed that at the time of retirement 
under VRS Sri C.P. Agarwal was holding the managerial and supervisory post and he was holding E-5 Grade at the 
time of his retirement and as such he had full power for all non-executive employees working under him in the matter 
of approve/confirmation of probationary period, to sanction advance for purchase of conveyance, to approve 
TA/LTC/LLTC/Medical Bills, to sanction advance for construction/purchase of land etc, sanction payment of 
gratuity, reimbursement of bills, issue show cause notice/warning imposing minor punishment. He has further stated 
that Sri C.P. Agarwal was not a workman. 


[भाग II—ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : सितम्बर 13, 2025/ ATE 22, 1947 6035 


In the cross-examination he has stated that the workman was Technical Personnel for which the 
candidates must have 3 years minimum experience after completing diploma, in any engineering stream. There is a 
line of promotion to that post and he was given power to take disciplinary action against certain staff, as per 
delegation. He has further deposed that the concerned workman joined in B.S. L as Construction Supervisor in the 
year 1970 the minimum qualification of the said post is Diploma Engineer. It is a technical post and in that line 
promotion is given. Post of Zonal Engineer is also a technical post. 


The management has filed the separation order of Sri C.P. Agarwal which has been marked as Exhibit 
M-3 and on its perusal it is evident that the designation of Sri C.P. Agarwal is Sr. Manager (M/M), GU and it 
transpires that he was released w.e.f. 31.03.2004 from service under VRS. On perusal of the back page of this 
document further it transpires that the designation of C.P. Agarwal is Senior Manager, his department is 
GU(M/M)(505), his date of joining is 24.11.1970, his date of release is 31.03.2004 and his pay scale/grade is 18500- 
23900-ES5 and his basic pay is Rs. 21650/-. On perusal of Exhibit M-6 which is document related to Delegation of 
Powers, Bokaro Steel Plant, it is evident that the employee with Senior Manager designation/equivalent (E-5) has full 
power for all non-executive employees regarding approval or confirmation to make arrangements for employees 
working under them against leave/tour, to approve reimbursement bills of photographs, uniform, stitching charges etc, 
to sanction advance of pay including Festival Advance, TA/LTC/LLTC etc, grant of sanction advance for 
construction/purchase of land/house/flat, Show cause notice/warning. 


14. The applicant C.P. Agarwal, in claim of his being a workman, has also cited the judgment of the case of S.K. 
Sharma Vs. Mahesh Chandra and Another in which the Hon’ble Apex Court has been pleased to hold that the 
designation or name of the post is not decisive and the nature of work is also to be considered while deciding the 
workman. 


In the present case the applicant C.P. Agarwal has claimed/deposed that he is a workman and he was 
initially appointed as Construction Supervisor and was last promoted to the post of Zonal Engineer which is also a 
technical post and he was not holding any supervisory or managerial post but in his entire evidence/pleading he has 
no where stated as to what work he was doing and what was the nature of his work. 


In recent case of Lenin Kumar Ray Vs. Express Publication (Madurai) Ltd. reported in 2024 SCC 
Online SC 2987), the Hon’ble Supreme Court has been pleased to hold that the law is well settled that the 
determinative factor for workman covered under section 2(s) of the I D Act is the principal duties and functions 
performed by an employee in the establishment and not merely the designation of his post and the onus of proving the 
nature of employment rests on the person claiming to be a workman within the definition of Section 2(s) of the LD. 
Act. 


Here in the instant case admittedly the employee C.P. Agarwal was appointed as Construction 
Supervisor and was promoted upto the post of Zonal Engineer which is purely a Managerial Post but the employee 
has no where disclosed as to what duty he was performing he has only stated generally that he was doing technical 
work which cannot be believed. 


Section 2(s) of the I.D. Act 1947 defines workman which reads as follows:- 


“workman” means any person (including an apprentice) employed in any industry to do any manual, 
unskilled, skilled, technical, operational, clerical or supervisory work for hire or reward, whether the terms of 
employment be express or implied, and for the purposes of any proceeding under this Act in relation to an industrial 
dispute, includes any such person who has been dismissed, discharged or retrenched in connection with, or as a 
consequence of, that dispute, or whose dismissal, discharge or retrenchment has led to that dispute, but does not 
include any such person- 


(1) who is subject to the Air Force Act, 1950 (45 of 1950), or the Army Act, 1950 (46 of 1950), or the Navy 
Act, 1957 (62 of 1957); or 


(ii) who is employed in the police service or as an officer or other employee of a person, or 
(iii) | who is employed mainly in a managerial or administrative capacity, or 


(iv) who, being employed in a supervisory capacity, draws wages exceeding one thousand six hundred rupees per 
mensem or exercises, either by the nature of the duties attached to the office or by reason of the powers vested in 
him, functions mainly of a managerial nature. 


15. Evidently, the employee was appointed as Construction Supervisor-II, and he was promoted to the post of 
Construction Supervisor Grade-I, Additional Divisional Engineer, Divisional Engineer and Zonal Engineer at the time 
of his Voluntary Retirement. As per Exhibit M-3 which is Separation Order (Release Order) of the employee C.P. 
Agarwal, he was working at the designation of Senior Manager at the time of VRS and his basic pay was Rs. 21650/- 
and he was enjoying Grade E-5 which is a Managerial Category Job as per Exhibit M-3 & Ext. M-6 and in the present 
case there is no specific document adduced by the applicant relating to the actual work and function performed by him 
and in the absence of any concrete material to demonstrate the nature of duties discharged by the applicant, this 


6036 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA :SEPTEMBER 13, 2025/BHADRA 22, 1947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


Tribunal is bound to take into consideration Exhibit M-3 and Exhibit M-6 adduced from the side of the employer and 
as per the same, the applicant was holding the designation of Senior Manager in Grade E at the time of his retirement 
under VRS which is a managerial and Administrative post and secondly, even otherwise, the applicant was admittedly 
employed in supervisory capacity and as per Ext W-5, Ext.M-3 and Ext.M-4 it is evident that he was drawing wages 
more than Rs. 1600 per month at the time of his retirement under VRS. 


16. On the basis of above appreciation of materials on record it is held that the applicant is not a workman as 
defined u/s 2(s) of the I.D. Act 1947 and so the provisions of I.D. Act, 1947 are not applicable to him. 


17. Now as regards the other two issues, as this Tribunal has held that the employee is not a workman u/s 2(s) of 
the I.D. Act, 1947, the tribunal has no jurisdiction to decide the other two issues. 


18. Considering above facts, circumstances and appreciation of evidence on record this Tribunal comes to the 
conclusion that the reference of the applicant C.P. Agarwal is not maintainable before this tribunal and is accordingly 
hereby dismissed on contest. Let the copies of Award in duplicate be sent to the Ministry of Labour & Employment, 
Government of India, New Delhi for information and notification. 


SACHINDRA KUMAR PANDEY, Presiding Officer 


नई दिल्‍ली, 8 सितम्बर, 2025
