as per above-referred clauses of the terms & conditions of the Contract. 


You are required to surrender the Company properties such as Identity Card, Uniform and other belongings, 
in your possession, immediately to the office. You accounts will be settled after checking your commitments, 
if any.” 
The main submission on behalf of the applicant/workman is that this termination order shows that it is 
stigmatic because this caused stigma, resulting into evil consequences, on the applicant/workman when it levels 
alleged serious misconduct on his part. 


The Learned Counsel has further submitted that the service contract was terminated levelling stigma 
without a proper departmental enquiry, hence, such termination of service agreement is vitiated in law. The Learned 
Counsel has submitted that the workman was not issued any charge sheet nor was he asked to appear before the 
enquiry officer, he was not given any opportunity to defend himself or cross-examine witness, he was not even 
granted name sake hearing. The respondents unilaterally declared in the impugned termination order the reply of the 
show cause notice as unsatisfactory without recording any reasons or objective evaluation. Thus, prejudice in the case 
of the applicant/workman also it has been submitted that this termination order is vitiated by procedural impropriety, 
non application of mind and legally informed. 


The Learned Counsel has referred to a judgment of Hon’ble Madhya Pradesh High Court in the case of 
Malkhan Singh Malviya v/s State of M.P., [2018] SCC Online MP 1774: ILR 2018 MP 660 in which it has been laid 
down that, “Even an employee not borne out of regular appointment is entitled to be afforded reasonable 
opportunity of being heard before the stigmatic order can be passed terminating his service and with mere issue of 
a show cause notice calling reply will not be sufficient in this respect.” 


On the other hand, it has been submitted that from the side of management that the workman was issued a 
show cause notice, he replied and after his reply was found unsatisfactory his service contract was terminated 
prematurely. Hence, there is no illegality in adopting the procedure. 


The Learned Counsel has referred to judgment of Hon’ble Supreme Court in the case of State of Uttar 
Pradesh v/s Smt. Sushmita reported in [2021] Volume IIIT SCC online 108, Para 14 and submitted that where no 
departmental enquiry is conducted, the employer retains the right to justify this itself before the court by leading 
evidence and that the absence of departmental enquiry does not itself vitiate the termination. 


Considering the arguments on this point submitted by parties as above, in light of record on this point, it 
comes out that the settled fact is that firstly, there was an exit clause in the service agreement providing one month 
notice or one month salary if any employee wishes to resign his service. This was unilaterally altered that the consent 
of the concerned employee who had executed the earlier service agreement and probably without duly informing him 
under the clause in service agreement providing sweeping powers to the management to alter the service conditions in 
the service agreement with respect to salary and otherwise. 


Even if the legality of the unilateral change in the service conditions is kept aside, it is established that before 
premature termination of service contract the workman was issued a notice by management levelling allegations and 
serious misconduct in form of intentionally insulting and use improper things for superior officer of the management 
by the applicant/workman. The show cause notice on record goes to show that he never admitted the charges, he 
simply stated, firstly, that according to service agreement executed between him and the management, his tenure is 
over, where is evidence with respect to the allegations and secondly, denying the allegations. The order of 
management regarding premature termination of service agreement has been referred earlier which goes to show that 
the service agreement was terminated because the workman was found to have committed serious act of misconduct. 


In the case of Purushottam Lal Dhingra v/s Union of India, reported in 1958 AIR 36, it has been laid down 
that a termination is stigmatic when it has evil consequences, the relevant paragraphs in this respect are being 
reproduced as follows:- 


“Art. 310 of the Constitution, which makes no distinction between persons holding permanent or 
temporary posts in the matter of their tenure being dependent on the pleasure of the President or the 


[भाग ए---खण्ड 361)] भारत का राजपत्र : सितम्बर 13, 2025/ भाद्र 22, 1947 6081 


Governor, Art. 311 which is in the nature of a proviso to Art.310, also makes no distinction between 
permanent and temporary posts and extends its protection equally to all Government servants holding 
permanent or temporary posts or officiating in any of them. 


But the protection of Art.311 can be available only where dismissal, removal or reduction in rank is sought to 
be inflicted by way of punishment and not otherwise. These were the major punishments evolved by the 
Service Rules and Rules of the Railway Code, and well-understood as such, against which protection was 
sought to be provided by the Rules. These protections were in due course incorporated in s. 240 of the 
Government of India Act, 1935, and reproduced in Art.311 of the Constitution, thus qualifying the principle 
embodied in Art. 310(1). 


One test for determining whether the termination of service was by way of punishment or otherwise is to 
ascertain whether under the Service Rules, but for such termination, the servant has the right to hold the 
post. In the three cases of (1) substantive appointment to a permanent post, (2) temporary appointment for 
a fixed term and (3) a temporary appointment which has ripened into a quasi-permanent status 
under the Temporary Service Rules, where such a right exists, the servant will be entitled to the protection 
of Art. 311. Conversely, where no such right can exist, as in the case of a probationary or officiating 
appointment to a permanent or temporary post or where the service has not ripened into a quasi-permanent 
status, and under the general law the service can be terminated on reasonable notice, the termination 
of service cannot amount to a punishment and attract the Article. Broadly speaking, Art. 311(2) can apply to 
those cases where the Government servant, if in private employment, could maintain an action for wrongful 
dismissal, removal or reduction in rank. So where the Government has, by contract, express or implied, or 
under the Rules, the right to terminate the service at any time, such termination, in the manner provided in 
the contract or under the Rules, cannot attract the provisions of Art. 311. That does not, however, means that 
the termination of service of a servant who has no right to the post can never be a dismissal or removal by 
way of punishment. Although in such a termination the actual motive of the Government must be wholly 
irrelevant, where it expressly chooses to penalise the servant for misconduct, negligence, inefficiency or the 
like by inflicting on him the punishment of dismissal, removal or reduction, the requirements of Art. 311 
must be complied with. 


A reduction in rank must, similarly, be a punishment if it carries penal consequences with it and the 
two tests to be applied are (1) whether the servant has a right to the post or the rank or (2) whether evil 
consequences such as forfeiture of pay or allowances, loss of seniority in his substantive rank, stoppage or 
postponement of future chances of promotion, follow as a result of the order. Where either of these tests 
applies, the reduction in rank mast be one within the meaning of Art. 311 (2) of the Constitution and attract 
its protection.” 


This judgment has been constantly followed by the Hon’ble Supreme Court and the Hon’ble High Courts, 
some case decisions are being referred to as follows:- 


1-Swati Priyadarshini Vs State of M.P. 2024 INSC 620 Held- 


“30. A bird’s eye views reveals thus. The appellant topped the revised Merit List, leading to her appointment 
as an APC. While serving as such, complaint(s) against her, in brief, were that she was not performing her 
duties, primarily on two counts — (i) not punctual in attending to her duties, and; (ii) not correctly reported 
with regard to the events in the hostel. As against these, the appellant’s response, via her replies to the SCNs, 
is that she, inter alia, frankly admits to being late on occasion, but to compensate for her late-coming, she 
used to sit till late evening in the office for completion of work. On this count, the Respondents cannot be 
faulted. It is no justification for the appellant to contend that she was late, but worked late/overtime such that 
the work did not suffer. However, as borne out from the record, with regard to the hostel, charge was given to 
her for only 5/6 days. As such, in our view, it cannot be said that within such a short period, the appellant, 
without fully understanding the attendant issues, could have 16 straightaway given any opinion/report on the 
hostel. 


Be that as it may, this case turns on our findings infra. 31. Clause 4 of the RGPSM’s General Service 
Conditions under the heading “Resignation/Termination” provides as below: 


“Persons working on contract can be terminated with one month notice if found inefficient. In case of 
persons found indulged in undesirable activities amounting to degradation of dignity of Mission, Mission 
Director shall reserve right to terminate him/her with immediate effect.” (emphasis supplied) 


32. Perusal of Clause 4 makes it clear that ordinarily, for inefficiency, one month’s notice is sufficient. The 
Clause also makes it clear that if someone is found to have indulged in “undesirable activities”, the Mission 


6082 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA :SEPTEMBER 13, 2025/BHADRA 22, 1947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


Director was competent to terminate such person’s services “with immediate effect”. We are afraid that the 
Respondents have placed themselves in a Catch-224 situation. If the order dated 30.03.2013 falls within the 
former part of Clause 4, as contended by the respondent, on the 4 colloquially, when one is placed in a 
dilemma due to two contradictory conditions. The phrase was popularized by Joseph Heller’s novel of the 
same name, first published in 1961.17premise that it is a case of termination simpliciter and non-stigmatic, 
then one month’s notice was required to be issued to the appellant, which admittedly was not done in the 
instant matter. 


Arguendo, were the order dated 30.03.2013 to be seen as falling under the latter part of Clause 4, it would be 
stigmatic, as made clear by the use of the words “indulged in undesirable activities amounting to degradation 
of dignity of Mission”. 


33. In either of the above-noted eventualities, the Impugned Judgment would have to necessarily be setaside. 
Nevertheless, let us examine the reasoning of the Division Bench, which opined that the order is non- 
stigmatic and simpliciter non-renewal of contract. The order dated 30.03.2013 was, quite obviously, the 
culmination of the process set into motion by the two SCNs, which has been overlooked by the Division 
Bench. The mere non-mention of the background situation or the SCNs in the order dated 30.03.2013 
cannot, by itself, be determinative of the nature of the order. As held by this Court in Samsher Singh v State 
of Punjab, (1974) 2 SCC 8315 and Anoop Jaiswal v Government of India, (1984) 2 SCC6, the form of an 
order is not its final determinant and the Court can find out the real reason and true character behind 
terminating/removing an employee. Moreover, the Impugned Judgment also does not deal with Clause 


३4. It is profitable to refer to what five learned Judges of this Court laid down in Parshotam Lal Dhingra v 
Union of India, 1957 SCC OnLine SC 5: 


“28. The position may, therefore, be summed up as follows: Any and every termination of service is not a 
dismissal, removal or reduction in rank. A termination of service brought about by the exercise of a 
contractual right is not per se dismissal or removal, as has been held by this Court in Satish Chander Anand 
v. Union of India [(1953) 1 SCC 420: (1953) SCR 655]. Likewise the termination of service by compulsory 
retirement in terms of a specific rule regulating the conditions of service is not tantamount to the infliction of 
a in the facts and circumstances of the case establish that an enquiry into allegations of serious and grave 
character of misconduct involving stigma has been made in infraction of the provision of Article 311 ...” 


“12. It is, therefore, now well settled that where the form of the order is merely a camouflage for an order of 
dismissal for misconduct it is always open to the court before which the order is challenged to go behind the 
form and ascertain the true character of the order. If the court holds that the order though in the form is 
merely a determination of employment is in reality a cloak for an order of punishment, the court would not 
be debarred, merely because of the form of the order, in giving effect to the rights conferred by law upon the 
employee.” punishment and does not attract Article 311(2), as has also been held by this Court in Shyam Lal 
7. State of Uttar Pradesh [(1955) 1 SCR 26]. In either of the two abovementioned cases the termination of the 
service did not carry with it the penal consequences of loss of pay, or allowances under Rule 52 of the 
Fundamental Rules. It is true that the misconduct, negligence, inefficiency or other disqualification may be 
the motive or the inducing factor which influences the Government to take action under the terms of the 
contract of employment or the specific service rule, nevertheless, if a right exists, under the contract or the 
rules, to terminate the service the motive operating on the mind of the Government is, as Chagla, C.J., has 
said in Shrinivas Ganesh v. Union of India [LR 58 Bom 673 : AIR (1956) Bom 455] wholly irrelevant. In 
short, if the termination of service is founded on the right flowing from contract or the service rules then, 
prima facie, the termination is not a punishment and carries with it no evil consequences and so Article 311 
is not attracted. But even if the Government has, by contract or under the rules, the right to terminate the 
employment without going through the procedure prescribed for inflicting the punishment of dismissal or 
removal or reduction in rank, the Government may, nevertheless, choose to punish the servant and if the 
termination of service is sought to be founded on misconduct, negligence, inefficiency or other 
disqualification, then it is a punishment and the requirements of Article 311 must be complied with. As 
already stated if the servant has got a right to continue in the post, then, unless the contract of employment 
or the rules provide to the 20 contrary, his services cannot be terminated otherwise than for misconduct, 
negligence, inefficiency or other good and sufficient cause. A termination of the service of such a servant on 
such grounds must be a punishment and, therefore, a dismissal or removal within Article 311, for it operates 
as a forfeiture of his right and he is visited with the evil consequences of loss of pay and allowances. It puts 
an indelible stigma on the officer affecting his future career. A reduction in rank likewise may be by way of 
punishment or it may be an innocuous thing. If the government servant has a right to a particular rank, then 
the very reduction from that rank will operate as a penalty, for he will then lose the emoluments and 
privileges of that rank. If, however, he has no right to the particular rank, his reduction from an officiating 
higher rank to his substantive lower rank will not ordinarily be a punishment. But the mere fact that the 
servant has no title to the post or the rank and the Government has, by contract, express or implied, or under 


[भाग Ive 361)] भारत का राजपत्र : सितम्बर 13, 2025/ भाद्र 22, 1947 6083 


the rules, the right to reduce him to a lower post does not mean that an order of reduction of a servant to a 
lower post or rank cannot in any circumstances be a punishment. The real test for determining whether the 
reduction in such cases is or is not by way of punishment is to find out if the order for the reduction also 
visits the servant with any penal consequences. Thus if the order entails or provides for the forfeiture of his 
pay or allowances or the loss of his seniority in his substantive rank or the stoppage or postponement of his 
future chances of promotion, then that circumstance may indicate that although in form the Government had 
purported to exercise its right to terminate the employment or to reduce the servant to a lower rank under the 
terms of the contract of employment or under the rules, in truth and reality the Government has terminated 
the employment as and by way of penalty. The use of the expression “terminate” or “discharge” is not 
conclusive. In spite of the use of such innocuous expressions, the court has to apply the two tests mentioned 
above, namely, (1) whether the servant had a right to the post or the rank, or (2) whether he has been visited 
with evil consequences of the kind hereinbefore referred to? If the case satisfies either of the two tests then it 
must be held that the servant has been punished and the termination of his service must be taken as a 
dismissal or removal from service or the reversion to his substantive rank must be regarded as a reduction in 
rank and if the requirements of the rules and Article 311, which give protection to government servant have 
not been complied with, the termination of the service or the reduction in rank must be held to be wrongful 
and in violation of the constitutional right of the servant.” (emphasis supplied) 


35. We would only be adding to verbosity by multiplying authorities. In view of the above dictum, it is clear that 
the Respondents did not comply with Clause 4 — either the first part or the second part thereof. The order dated 
30.03.2013 does visit the appellant with evil consequences and would create hurdles for her further 
employment.” 


Hence on the basis of the said preposition of law as referred to above action of management in prematurely 
terminating the service agreement of the applicant/workman on the charges of misconduct without 
conducting a departmental enquiry is held vitiated in law. 


As regards argument from the side of management that they should be given an opportunity to prove the 
charge before this Tribunal, this argument cannot be accepted because it is a fixed term contract, the time limit of 
which is expired and secondly, the management has nowhere pleaded that if the enquiry is held vitiated, they be given 
opportunity to prove the charges before this Tribunal. Accordingly, the judgment of State of Uttar Pradesh v/s Smt. 
Sushmita reported in [2021] Volume HI SCC online 108, Para 14 referred to by management does not help them. 


As regards the relief to which the applicant/workman may be entitled, following facts are relevant in this 
respect which is being referred as follows:- 


(i) The service contract of the applicant/workman was unilaterally changed by management to the prejudice 
of the workman by taking away his right to spot resignation the legality of which is suspect. 


(ii) The premature termination of service contract order was issued against the workman just less than a 
week before the completion of service tenure mentioned in his service agreement. 


(iii) The applicant had to approach Hon’ble High Court by way of filing W.P. No. 22050/2023 and he could 
get relieving order only after Hon’ble High Court directed the management to issue it vide its order 
dated 02.09.2023. 


(iv) During the proceedings of this case before this Tribunal, it came out that the applicant/workman was 
ready to withdraw his case if the management changed the terminology used by it in the termination 
order by way of deleting portion of the termination order which is stigmatic against him, but the 
management did not consent to it. 


The applicant/workman has been out of job due to his stigmatic termination without enquiry till date and his 
future prospects are ruined. 


Since it was a fixed term appointment the tenure of which has already expired, it will not be in the interest of 
justice to order the reinstatement of the applicant/workman. It is also noteworthy that the applicant/workman himself 
has resigned prematurely in this case on this ground also his reinstatement will not be a just relief. 


Keeping in view the fact that he has lost job opportunity due to his this termination order and has suffered 
harassment for more than two years, lump sum compensation in lieu of all his losses and claims seems to be a just 
relief for him. Keeping in view all the facts and circumstances of the case in hand, a lump sum compensation of Rs. 
15,00,000/- (Fifteen Lacs) in lieu of all his losses will meet the ends of justice which he is held entitled to receive 
from the management within 60 days from the date of publication award in Gazette failing which interest @ 10% p.a. 
from the date of award till payment. 


In the light of above discussion and findings the reference is answered as follows:- 
AWARD 


“Holding the action of management of AI Airport Service Limited in terminating the service of its 
workman Anuroop Pandey without conducting proper disciplinary proceedings on the ground that the workman 


6084 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA :SEPTEMBER 13, 2025/BHADRA 22, 1947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


was a fixed term employee unjust, illegal and arbitrary, the workman is held entitled to lump sum compensation of 
Rs. 15,00,000/- (Fifteen Lacs), payable to him by management within 60 days from the date of publication award 
in Gazette failing which interest @ 10% p.a. from the date of award till payment.” 


Copy of this judgment be sent to the Ministry as per rules for publication in official Gazette. 
DATE:- 18/07/2025 
P.K. SRIVASTAVA, Presiding Officer 


नई दिल्‍ली, 8 सितम्बर, 2025

सरकार भिलाई स्टील प्लांट के प्रबंधतंत्र के संबद्ध नियोजकों और श्रीमती जयंती के बीच अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट केन्द्रीय सरकार

गया है, प्रकाशित करती है जो केन्द्रीय सरकार को सॉफ्ट कॉपी के साथ 08.09.2025 को प्राप्त हुआ था |

Workman 
Vs 
The Managing Director, 
Bhilai Steel Plant, 
PO - Bhilai, Distt. Durg (CG) 
Management 
(JUDGMENT) 


(Passed on this 24" day of July - 2025) 


As per letter dated 05/12/2014 by the Government of India, Ministry of Labour, New Delhi, the reference 
has been made to this Tribunal under Section-10 of Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (in short the ‘Act’) as per 
Notification No. L-29012/24/2014-IR(M) dt. 05/12/2014. The dispute under reference relates to: 


(MTT वा--खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : सितम्बर 13, 2025/ ATE 22, 1947 6085 


“Whether the action of the Management of Bhilai, in terminating the services of Smt. Jayanti 
W/o Late N. Subramanayam, w.e.f. 07-07-2012 is legal and justified? If not, what relief the Workman is 
entitled to?” 


Case of the workman is that, she was working as Staff Attendant Grade-6 in the General Establishment 
Department of the Management. She was removed from service by the Management vide order dated 07.07.2012, she 
came to know about this order when she obtained information from Management under right to information Act vide 
letter of Management dated 07.10.2013. It is further her case that, she became ill and was ousted by her son from 
house, she became mentally disturbed and started living with her daughter at the residence of her daughter which is 
House No.6 A Road No. 37B Sector -7 Bhilai, Distt. Durg (CG). She had furnished written as well oral information to 
the Management with respect to her new address. She further alleges that none of the letters said to be sent to her by 
the Management namely. She received no information about the charge sheet regarding enquiry, regarding show 
cause notice said to be issued by the Disciplinary Authority that enquiry report nor did she receive any punishment 
order. The Management removed her from service on the ground of her absence at Workplace from 10.05.2010 to 
13.08.2010 without any intimation and without any enquiry giving her opportunity to defend herself which is unjust, 
illegal and arbitrary. She raised a dispute before the concerned Labour Commissioner after failure of conciliation, the 
reference was made to this Tribunal. The Workman has prayed that, holding the order of Management dated 
07.07.2013 removing her from service, she be held entitled to be reinstated with back wages and benefits. 


The case of the Management is mainly that, she was first appointed by Management w.e.f. 25.10.1986 on 
compassionate ground as Kitchen Attendant. She was designated as staff attendant, was irregular in presence at 
Workplace. She was in the habit of remaining absent from duty unauthorizedly and without any leave permission or 
sanctioned leave, she was allotted quarter No. 3C Street No. 6 Sector 8 Bhilai by Management by virtue of her 
employment, she did not vacant the residence allotted dispute termination of services. She was required under Clause 
17 of Certified Standing Orders to inform the Management about the change of her residential address which she 
never did. She was issued a charge-sheet dated 10.09.2010 and 27.05.2011 for unauthorized and willfully absence of 
71 and 207 days and after enquiry was punished with reduction in increments. Her unauthorized absence from 
January 2009 to June 2012 was about 1049 days. She was present only for 114 days during this period on workplace. 
She was issued a charge sheet dated 22.12.2011 for unauthorized and willful absence, for a period of 207 days. 
Management decided to conduct an enquiry vide order dated 19.10.2012, the moments with respect to enquiry was 
sent on her residential address, she did not appear in the enquiry on any of the days, the notice with respect to the 
enquiry was sent by way of registered post as well by way of fixing the notice on the General Notice Board. The 
Enquiry Officer conducted enquiry and submitted his enquiry report holding the charge of the misconduct against her, 
by way of unauthorizedly and willfully absenting herself proved. The punishment order was passed by the 
disciplinary authority after giving her opportunity to show cause. 


The Workman has filed a rejoinder wherein she has mainly re-treated her case. 
On the basis of pleadings, following issues were frame vide order dated 29.03.2017. 
1. Whether enquiry conducted against the workman is proper and legal? 


2. Whether the charges alleged against workman are proved from evidence adduced in enquiry 
proceeding? 


3. Whether the punishment of dismissal imposed against the Workman is legal? 
4. If so, to what relief the workman is entitled? 
Issue No. 1 - 


Issue No. 1 was taken as a preliminary issue and was decided on the basis of evidence on record 
vide order dated 09.09.2024 holding the Departmental enquiry just legal and proper. This order is part of this 
judgment. 


None of the parties filed any other evidence on remaining issues. 


At the time of arguments, none appeared for the Workman. Argument of Learned Counsel for Management 
Mr. R.C. Shrivastava were heard by me. Later on, Mr. Ram Raj Chouhan Learned Counsel for Workman submitted 
his arguments on 14.05.2025. He written arguments also, which are part of the record. I have gone through the written 
arguments as well the record. 


Issue No. | is answered accordingly. 
Issue No. 2 - 


The settled preposition of law with respect to proof of the charge in a Departmental Enquiry is that, the 
charge not be proved beyond doubt. 


Perusal of enquiry papers filed and proved by Management, it comes out that, during enquiry, witness have 


6086 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA :SEPTEMBER 13, 2025/BHADRA 22, 1947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


supported on oath that documents the absence of the Workman are according to them was unauthorized because, she 
absented herself without informing the Management, and without getting any leave sanctioned. The reasons behind 
her absence were to be proved by the Workman, in which she has failed. Hence, the findings of the enquiry officer 
and congruence of the Disciplinary Authority with respect to proof of charge is held to have recorded on the basis of 
evidence and is affirmed. 


Issue No. 2 is answered accordingly. 
Issue No. 3 - 


There is evidence on record that, on two occasions earlier the same charge was proved against the 
workman and she was awarded punishment. It goes to show that, she is a habitual in committing this type of 
misconduct in the light of these facts, the punishment of her removal from service by Management is held 
proportionate to the charge proved. 


Issue No. 3 is answered accordingly. 
Issue No. 4 - 
In the light of above discussion and findings, the Workman is held entitled to no relief. 
Issue No. 4 is answered accordingly. 
On the light of above discussion, the reference is answered as follows: 
AWARD 


Holding the action of Management of Bhilai Steel Plant, in terminating services of the Workman Smt. 
Jayanti wife of Late N. Subaramanyam w.e.f. from 07.07.2012 legal and justified, she is held entitled to no 
relief. 


No order as to cost. 
DATE:- 24/07/2025 


P.K. SRIVASTAVA, Presiding Officer 


नई दिल्‍ली, 8 सितम्बर, 2025

का.आ. 1643.--औद्योगिक विवाद अधिनियम, 1947 (1947 का 14) की धारा 17 के अनुसरण में, केन्द्रीय 
सरकार भिलाई इस्पात प्लांट; शिक्षा विभाग; बी.एस.पी. हाई स्कूल के प्रबंधतंत्र के संबद्ध नियोजकों और श्रीमती अहिल्या 
बाई पत्नी तेलुचंद्र, बी.एस.पी. उच्चतर माध्यमिक विद्यालय के बीच अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट केन्द्रीय सरकार औद्योगिक अधिकरण 
एवं श्रम न्यायालय, जबलपुर, पंचाट (रिफरेन्स न.-15/2022) को जैसा कि अनुलग्रक में दिखाया गया है, प्रकाशित करती 
है जो केन्द्रीय सरकार को सॉफ्ट कॉपी के साथ 08.09.2025 को प्राप्त हुआ था | 


[सं. जेड-16025/04/2025-आईआर(एम)-136] 


सलोनी, उप निदेशक

AWARD 
(Passed on this 057 day of August-2025.) 


The workman has filed petition u/s. 2A (2&3) of the Industrial Disputes Act 1947, as amended by 
Amendment Act, 2010 (in short the ‘Act’). 


[भाग ए---खण्ड 361)] भारत का राजपत्र : सितम्बर 13, 2025/ भाद्र 22, 1947 6091 


The case of the workman is that she is an employee of B.S.P. High School which is run by Management of 
Bhilai Steel Plant and is under their control as well supervision. She was first engaged as sweeper on 01.01.1999 and 
was paid wages till 02.11.2020 @ Rs. 650/- per month. She was disengaged from services, she filed a petition before 
the Labour Court. The Labour Court passed an order on 29.01.2005 directing the reinstatement with 50% backwages. 
This judgment and award of the Labour Court was challenged by the management before Hon’ble High Court of 
Chhatisgarh at Bilaspur. Hon’ble High Court remanded the matter back to the Lower Court to decide the matter afresh 
in light of directions mentioned in the judgment. According to the workman, the Labour Court passed this judgment 
and award on 05.12.2011 which was not complied with by the management. The workman had to file a contempt 
petition before the Hon’ble High Court of Chhatisgarh which directed execution of the said award through the Labour 
Commissioner and payment be made to the workman on minimum wages since 01.12.2015. Since then, she has been 
working but management issued a letter dated 02.07.2020 stating that since the school will not function in the 
academic calendar 2020-21, hence her services were not required. They paid her one month salary and compensation 
through bank cheque. According to workman, this action of management is arbitrary because other staff were 
transferred to different schools run by the same management and she was retrenched, the workman as prayed that 
management of the school be directed to reinstate her with all back wages and benefits. 


Management of the Bhilai Steel Plant has taken a case that there is no employer-employee relation between 
the parties. The school is not under the management supervision or control of Bhilai Steel Plant and this Tribunal does 
not have the jurisdiction to entertain the dispute. 


The workman filed photocopy of certificates issued by Labour Commissioner, Judgment of Labour Court, 
Durg, and Judgment of Hon’ble High Court of Chhatisgarh in contempt proceedings. 


The workman absented thereafter hence the case proceeded Ex-parte against her. 
None of the parties filed any evidence or affidavit in evidence. 


I have heard argument of learned Counsel for management Mr. R. C. Shrivastava. None appeared for the 
workman. I have gone through the record as well. 


The initial burden to prove its case is on the petitioner/workman she has to prove that the school was under 
controlled supervision of management of Bhilai Steel Plant and she was engaged by Bhilai Steel Plant, in absence of 
evidence on these facts, holding the claim of the workman not proved, the petition deserves to be dismissed. 


ORDER 
Petition dismissed. 
DATE: 05/08/2025 
P.K. SRIVASTAVA, Presiding Officer 


नई दिल्‍ली, 8 सितम्बर, 2025
